## THE THREE LAWS OF THIS BUILD

```
LAW 1 — PRESERVE & HARMONISE
Preserve all existing features. Merge overlapping ones into unified, fully working hubs.
Nothing is removed. Nothing is deprecated. Nothing is simplified.

LAW 2 — FIX & ACTIVATE
Every broken, static, or non-functional component must be fully repaired and connected.
No dead clicks. No empty buttons. No placeholder tabs. No blank pages. Ever.

LAW 3 — ELEVATE TO PRODUCTION GRADE
Every element must be interactive, responsive, animated where appropriate,
scalable, secure, accessible, and production-ready.
```

---

## NON-NEGOTIABLE DESIGN CONSTRAINTS

```
CONSTRAINT 1: No ranking, score, or leaderboard may be driven by follower counts,
              likes, view counts, shares, or any derivative of attention metrics.

CONSTRAINT 2: No feature may exist solely to increase time-on-platform or
              posting frequency. Every feature must map to the ecosystem loop.

CONSTRAINT 3: Every trust-bearing number shown to any user, recruiter, or
              institution must be explainable on demand with a "How this is
              calculated" view — built before the number ships.
```

---

## 1. PLATFORM IDENTITY & POSITIONING

### 1.1 What PROFILE Is

**PROFILE is a Global Professional Trust Operating System (PTOS).**

It is not a social network. Not a job board. Not a portfolio builder. Not a resume site.
It is the infrastructure layer for verified professional identity, contribution, competence, trust, and opportunity — functioning as an operating system for professional credibility.

**Positioning Statement:**
> *"PROFILE is a Professional Identity Operating System. It takes a person's work and turns it into evidence, turns evidence into verification, turns verification into trust, and turns trust into opportunity."*

### 1.2 What PROFILE Is Not

- NOT optimised for: virality · outrage · vanity metrics · attention farming · algorithmic addiction
- NOT comparable to or competing with any named platform
- NOT a storage repository, portfolio display, or social feed

**PROFILE is infrastructure. Infrastructure does not compete. It enables.**

### 1.3 Approved Identity Statements

Use consistently across all surfaces, copy, and marketing:

```
"Verified Work. Trusted Identity."
"Built on Proof."
"Where Work Becomes Credibility."
"Trust Earned. Opportunity Unlocked."
"The Infrastructure of Professional Trust."
"Identity Through Contribution."
"Professional Identity Powered by Evidence."
"One Identity. Infinite Opportunities."
"Build Your Identity. Verify Your Value."
```

### 1.4 The Ecosystem Loop

Every module must feed into this loop at no fewer than one point. Modules that cannot point to a step in this loop do not belong in the product.

| Stage | What Happens | Primary Module |
|---|---|---|
| **Identity** | User establishes verifiable professional identity | Identity & Onboarding |
| **Evidence** | Work, learning, and recognition logged as structured records | Professional Activity Layer |
| **Verification** | Third parties confirm the evidence is real | Verification Engine (VXI) |
| **Trust** | Verified evidence accumulates into transparent, auditable score | Trust Score & Trust Passport |
| **Discovery** | Recruiters, institutions, and peers find by trust signal | Discovery Engine |
| **Opportunity** | Discovery converts into offers, scholarships, and contracts | Opportunity Hub |
| **Growth** | Outcomes feed back into evidence base, restarting the loop | Analytics & Career Readiness |

### 1.5 Closing Vision

> *"Every user should leave their first session with one conviction: My work matters here. My identity can be trusted here. My achievements can be verified here. My opportunities can grow here."*

## 2. TECHNOLOGY STACK & ARCHITECTURE

### 2.1 Core Stack

```yaml
Framework:        React + TypeScript
Build Pipeline:   Vite or Next.js
                  # NO browser-side Babel CDN in production
                  # All JS assets self-contained
Animations:       Framer Motion (component transitions + reveals)
SVG Animations:   Native CSS + GSAP (trust graphs, identity nodes, backgrounds)
Styling:          Tailwind CSS or CSS Modules
Scroll Triggers:  Intersection Observer API
State:            Zustand or Redux Toolkit
Routing:          React Router v6 (with guards)
Offline:          Service Workers + IndexedDB
PWA:              manifest.json + installable
```

### 2.2 Viewport Support

```
Mobile (320px–767px) · Tablet (768px–1023px) · Laptop (1024px–1439px)
Desktop (1440px–1919px) · Ultra-Wide (1920px+)
```

### 2.3 Responsive Requirements

```css
/* All layout containers — NO fixed pixel dimensions */
layout: CSS Grid + Flexbox
typography: CSS clamp() for fluid scaling
viewport: svh, dvh, cqi modern units
animations: GPU-accelerated (transform, opacity only)
images: WebP + AVIF with responsive srcset
order: Mobile-first layout
```

### 2.4 Accessibility (WCAG 2.2 AA Minimum)

```
✓ Full keyboard navigation on all interactive elements
✓ Focus-visible states (not just :focus, use :focus-visible)
✓ Reduced-motion media query on ALL animations
✓ ARIA labels on all icons, badges, and interactive regions
✓ Screen-reader compatible semantic HTML structure
✓ Colour contrast ratio ≥ 4.5:1 for text
✓ Skip navigation links on all pages
```
// This is the single accessibility target for the whole product — the same standard
// applies to navigation (§10.1) and every other surface. Nothing here is optional.

### 2.5 Performance Targets

```
First Contentful Paint:          < 1.5s
Time to Interactive:             < 3.0s
Animation:                       60fps maintained
API response (p95):              < 200ms
CV auto-update after activity:   < 2s
Trust score update after trigger: < 1s
Lighthouse Performance:          > 90
Lighthouse Accessibility:        > 95
Lighthouse Best Practices:       > 95
Lighthouse SEO:                  > 95
```

## 3. GLOBAL DESIGN SYSTEM

> **BRAND GUIDE STATUS:** Subsections 3.1, 3.2, 3.4, and 3.5 below reflect the Official
> PROFILE Brand Guide, Final Edition v2.0 — determined by reconciling three earlier,
> conflicting brand proposals against the actual logo asset (pixel-sampled) and the actual
> built `PROFILE_Brand_Motion_Complete.html` motion prototype (parsed from its real CSS
> variables and keyframes), rather than by preference among the proposals. Subsections 3.3,
> 3.6, and 3.7 are unaffected and remain exactly as originally specified. See §3.13 for the
> full version history and rationale.

### 3.1 Colour Palette

```css
/* ── Primary (confirmed against pixel-sampled logo: dominant blue #0064DC) ── */
--color-royal-blue:  #0057D9;   /* primary — logo, buttons, links, active nav, main CTAs */
--color-deep-blue:   #003A8C;   /* secondary — headers, hero gradients, enterprise, PROFILE OS branding */

/* ── Trust Accent (NEW — corrects all three prior proposals) ── */
--color-trust-cyan:  #00E5FF;   /* Trust Score, Trust Passport, Trust Center, verification-in-progress —
                                    sourced directly from the logo's gradient sphere and the built
                                    motion prototype's --cyan token. Retires the unused purple
                                    Trust color and never overlaps with Success or Gold. */

/* ── Achievement Accent (restricted use — max ~3% of any screen) ── */
--color-gold:        #F4B400;   /* premium indicators, awards, featured/sponsored labels, top-tier
                                    badges ONLY — never trust, never verification, never warning */

/* ── Human Accent (NEW — restricted use, max ~3% of any screen, same discipline as Gold) ── */
--color-warmth:      #FF6F59;   /* Warm Coral. The one color in this system whose job is to make
                                    someone feel welcomed, not evaluated: first-login greeting,
                                    "you did it" moments (first activity recorded, first badge,
                                    first opportunity received), empty-state illustrations. Never
                                    used for trust, verification, or achievement — those already
                                    have Trust Cyan and Gold. A platform built entirely from blue,
                                    cyan, and navy is credible; it is not, on its own, warm. This
                                    is the one deliberate exception, used exactly as sparingly as
                                    Gold, so it still means something the one time a day it appears. */

/* ── Neutrals ── */
--color-white:       #FFFFFF;
--color-graphite:    #1E293B;   /* primary text, icons, borders, dark-mode surfaces */
--color-black:       #0A0E1A;   /* high-contrast accessibility mode, print only — a near-black
                                    navy matching the built prototype's true background tone,
                                    not pure #000000 */

/* ── Semantic ── */
--color-success:     #00C853;   /* matches the built motion prototype's tested --green token */
--color-warning:     #F59E0B;   /* now fully distinct from Gold — no more double-duty */
--color-danger:      #DC2626;
--color-info:        #0EA5E9;
--color-trust:       var(--color-trust-cyan);   /* alias — Trust and Trust Cyan are the same color */

/* ── Gradients ── */
--gradient-primary:  linear-gradient(135deg, var(--color-royal-blue), var(--color-deep-blue));
--gradient-trust:    linear-gradient(135deg, var(--color-deep-blue), var(--color-trust-cyan));
```

### 3.2 Typography

```css
/* Primary — unanimous across every brand proposal reviewed */
font-family-primary:    'Inter', system-ui, sans-serif;

/* Monospace — Trust IDs, verification hashes, copyright codes, code samples */

/* Fluid Scale (clamp: min · preferred · max) — unchanged */
--text-xs:    clamp(0.75rem,  0.7rem  + 0.25vw, 0.875rem);
--text-sm:    clamp(0.875rem, 0.8rem  + 0.375vw, 1rem);
--text-base:  clamp(1rem,     0.9rem  + 0.5vw,   1.125rem);
--text-lg:    clamp(1.125rem, 1rem    + 0.625vw,  1.25rem);
--text-xl:    clamp(1.25rem,  1.1rem  + 0.75vw,   1.5rem);
--text-2xl:   clamp(1.5rem,   1.25rem + 1.25vw,   2rem);
--text-3xl:   clamp(1.875rem, 1.5rem  + 1.875vw,  2.5rem);
--text-4xl:   clamp(2.25rem,  1.75rem + 2.5vw,    3.5rem);
--text-hero:  clamp(2.5rem,   2rem    + 3vw,       4.5rem);
```

### 3.6 Animated Background Pattern System

**Applied to:** auth screens · onboarding · main app (behind cards)

```css
/* Professional icon pattern — CSS Grid, fixed position */
.bg-pattern {
  position: fixed;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: var(--z-background);
  pointer-events: none;
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(80px, 1fr));
  opacity: never exceeds 0.25; /* HARD LIMIT */
}

/* Icon set */
/* 👥 💼 🔗 📁 🌐 🧠 📞 💬 ⭐ 🧑‍💻 🏢 📊 🪪 📣 🧩 ✅ 🔐 🏆 📝 🤝 */

/* Transition smoothly between dark/light mode */
transition: opacity 0.3s ease, filter 0.3s ease;
```

### 3.6a Human Representation & Photography

```
PROFILE's entire premise is that people are seen as they actually are, not as a polished
performance. The visual language has to keep that promise before a single word of copy does.

REQUIRED:
✓ Real, diverse, unretouched-looking human photography wherever a person is shown in
  marketing, onboarding illustration, or empty-state art — genuine range of age, ethnicity,
  ability, and profession, weighted toward the vocational and self-taught professionals
  this document insists are equal citizens (§8.3), not just polished office headshots
✓ Default avatars (no photo uploaded) use initials on a solid brand-token color, never a
  generic silhouette icon — a placeholder should still feel like it belongs to a specific
  person, not a category
✓ Illustration style, where used, is geometric and abstract (consistent with the
  identity-node / trust-graph visual language in §6.4) — never cartoon mascots, never
  corporate-stock-photo body language (exaggerated laptop laughter, staged handshakes)

FORBIDDEN:
✗ Any photography that reads as generic stock imagery — this is the fastest possible way
  to undermine a platform whose core claim is authenticity
✗ Airbrushing or beauty-filtering guidance of any kind in product photography
```

### 3.6b First-Visit and Second-Visit Design Principles

```
These are two different psychological moments and PROFILE designs for them differently,
not with the same screen twice.

FIRST VISIT (Hero, §6) — the job is trust, established fast, without asking for anything:
✓ The Hero's own Logo Animation Sequence (§6.3) already includes a `VerificationPulse`
  phase — that pulse, not a numeric widget, is the Hero's signature moment. A Trust Score
  ring showing an actual number belongs to the authenticated experience (§6.4's Hard Rule
  correctly keeps the Hero itself to six focal elements, nothing more); the full
  `TRUST_SCORE_RING` (§33.2) becomes someone's own signature moment the first time it
  animates in with their real score, not before.
✓ Confident, unhurried motion (§33.1) — no countdown timers, no "3 people viewed this
  today" urgency manufacturing, no auto-playing video with sound. A platform asking to be
  trusted with someone's professional evidence does not behave like it is desperate for
  their attention.
✓ Nothing before the first meaningful click requires a decision harder than "Create
  Profile" or "Sign In" (§6.5) — every other question waits.

SECOND VISIT AND BEYOND (Home, §11 + Daily Return Engine, §11.0) — the job is to make a
returning person feel recognized and visibly further along, not reset to zero:
✓ The Trust Score ring's animation on a return visit shows the delta since last visit
  (§33.2's strokeDashoffset animates from the previous score, not from zero every time) —
  growth should be visibly felt, not just numerically reported
✓ The Daily Return Engine's greeting (§11.0) and its 2–4 recommendations are the first
  thing rendered, above the activity feed — a returning person's actual next step matters
  more than showing them everyone else's activity first
✓ Warm Coral (§3.1) is permitted here, exactly once per session at most — a first-activity
  milestone, a badge earned, an opportunity received — never for routine, everyday actions
```

### 3.7 Interactive State Rules (Global — Zero Exceptions)

```typescript
// Every interactive element MUST implement all states:
interface InteractiveStates {
  button:  ['default', 'hover', 'focus', 'pressed', 'loading', 'success', 'error', 'disabled'];
  tab:     ['default', 'hover', 'active', 'selected', 'disabled'];
  input:   ['default', 'focus', 'valid', 'invalid', 'filled', 'disabled'];
  card:    ['default', 'hover'];   // hover: elevation + scale(1.01)
  modal:   ['open', 'close'];      // smooth backdrop + transform
  icon:    ['default', 'hover', 'focus', 'touch'];
}
```

### 3.8 Logo Usage

```
ASSET:         Single mark — rounded ribbon letterform enclosing a person silhouette,
               blue-to-cyan gradient. Source: /assets/brand/logo.svg
FORMAT:        SVG only in every product context; high-res PNG/PDF acceptable for print only
MINIMUM SIZE:  24px height (icon contexts), 32px (navigation/headers)
CLEAR SPACE:   Minimum 0.25× logo height on all sides
VARIANTS:      Full gradient (default) · Monochrome white (dark surfaces) ·
               Monochrome graphite (light, low-emphasis contexts)
NEVER:         Stretch, recolor outside approved variants, add drop shadows, rotate,
               or place on a busy background without a solid/blurred backing
FILES:         /assets/brand/logo.svg, /assets/brand/logo-mono-white.svg,
               /assets/brand/logo-mono-graphite.svg
```

### 3.9 Colour Usage Ratios

| Colour | Purpose | Approx. Usage |
|---|---|---|
| Royal Blue | Primary actions, links, core branding | 30% |
| Deep Blue | Headers, navigation, enterprise surfaces | 15% |
| White / light surfaces | Backgrounds, content areas | 30% |
| Graphite | Text and icons | 15% |
| Trust Cyan | Trust Score, verification, Trust Center accents | 7% |
| Gold | Achievement, premium, featured highlights only | 3% |
| Warm Coral | First-login, milestone, and celebration moments only (§3.6b) | <1% |

Warm Coral's ceiling is deliberately the smallest in the system — smaller than Gold's,
not additional to it. If it starts showing up often enough to need its own real
percentage, it has already stopped meaning "this moment is special."

### 3.10 Sub-Brand Application — PROFILE vs. PROFILE OS

```
PROFILE (consumer brand, www.myprofile.com)
  Primary palette:  Royal Blue + White + Trust Cyan
  Feel:             Accessible, personal, professional growth

PROFILE OS (technology brand, www.profileos.com)
  Primary palette:  Deep Blue + Graphite, with restrained Gold accents only
                     on enterprise/premium-tier documentation
  Feel:             Infrastructure, security, APIs, institutional trust
```

Both sub-brands share one logo, one typography system, and one semantic colour set — the
distinction is emphasis, never a divergent identity.

### 3.11 Brand Motion System

The logo's animated states are implemented and tested in `PROFILE_Brand_Motion_Complete.html`.
This subsection documents that system as authoritative — every timing value below is read
directly from the built prototype's own keyframes, not estimated.

```
LOADING / IDENTITY VERIFYING (default active state)
  Keyframes: dot-pulse (2s ease-in-out infinite) · glow-breathe (2s ease-in-out infinite)
             arc-wave (2s cubic-bezier) · arc-grow (1.8s cubic-bezier)
             orbit-spin (1.4s linear infinite) · process-spin (1s linear infinite)
             scan-rotate (2.5s linear infinite)
  Meaning:   Identity connecting → Trust propagating → Verification in progress →
             Opportunity loading. This is PROFILE's official loading animation
             platform-wide, replacing generic spinners.

IDLE / OFFLINE
  Keyframes: glow-dim (3s ease-in-out infinite) · glow-idle (4s ease-in-out infinite)
  Meaning:   The head remains fully visible; trust signals dim and slow rather than
             disappearing. Reconnect returns directly to the Loading state.

ERROR
  Keyframes: shake-logo (.65s cubic-bezier, single play) · error-bg (2s ease-in-out infinite)
             error-ring (2s ease-in-out infinite)
  Meaning:   A single brief shake acknowledges failure, then settles into a slow
             error-ring pulse in Danger red (#DC2626) — never a static broken state.
             An error state always implies a visible retry path nearby.

WARNING
  Keyframe:  amber-flash (2s ease-in-out)
  Meaning:   Expiring credentials or pending-attention states, in Warning amber (#F59E0B),
             visually distinct from Error.

SUCCESS
  Keyframes: success-ring (2.5s ease-out infinite) · check-draw (2.5s ease infinite)
  Meaning:   The trust ring completes and glows in Success green (#00C853); a checkmark
             draws on. Official confirmation animation — verification approved, upload
             complete, application submitted.

GOVERNING RULE
  Never show failure as a final, static state. Every error animation resolves toward a
  visible recovery action — shake-logo always settles into error-ring, which always
  pairs with a retry affordance in the surrounding component.
```

### 3.13 Brand Guide Version History & Rationale

| Version | Change |
|---|---|
| Original (this section, pre-Brand Guide) | First documented palette — Royal Blue #2563EB, Gold-based accent and warning, purple Trust color |
| Brand Guide v1.0 (two independent drafts) | Proposed Royal Blue #0057D9, Gold #F4B400 as primary accent |
| Developer Guide brand motion spec | Proposed Royal Blue #0057B8, documented intended motion states in prose |
| **Final Edition v2.0 (this document, current)** | Reconciled all three against the actual logo file and the actual built motion prototype; corrected Gold → Trust Cyan as the trust accent; adopted the prototype's tested dark-mode surface system and real animation timings as authoritative |

### 3.14 Universal Page Layout Contract

```
Every page in the product, regardless of module, must implement:
✓ Breadcrumb or back-navigation, consistent with Section 10's navigation model
✓ A visible page title, using the existing typography scale (§3.2)
✓ Secondary actions placed consistently (top-right on desktop, overflow menu on mobile)
✓ Persistent access to primary navigation (§10.1 bottom tabs / §10.6 desktop sidebar) —
  no page may trap the user without a way back to Home, Search, or the Profile Drawer
✓ Full keyboard and touch accessibility per §2.4 (WCAG 2.2 AA)
✓ Skeleton loading states, never raw spinners, per §33.1

No new design tokens are introduced by this contract — it reuses existing spacing (§3.3),
typography (§3.2), and animation tokens (§33.2) exactly as they already exist.
```

## 4. SCHEMA DEFINITIONS

### 4.1 Core User Schema

```typescript
interface User {
  // Identity
  pid:              string;           // PRF-9X7K-24AB-MQ8D — immutable, never reused
  username:         string;           // @handle — globally unique
  accountType:      'individual' | 'organisation';
  pathway?:         'professional' | 'competence' | 'hybrid'; // individual only — §8.3 category, WHAT kind of professional
  careerStage?:      CareerStage;                              // individual only — WHERE in their journey, evolves over time, §50.9
  careerStageHistory?: CareerStageTransition[];                 // individual only — preserved milestones on transition, never overwritten, §50.9.1
  journeyStage?:     string;                                    // individual only — finer-grained sub-stage within an academic or
                                                                 // independent-craft careerStage (e.g. "Year 1", "PhD — Final Stage",
                                                                 // "Just starting out"); set once at onboarding (§8.8.1), editable anytime
                                                                 // in Settings as a person progresses. Value set and routing table: §50.9.5a.

  // Names
  fullLegalName:    string;
  professionalName?: string;
  nativeLanguageName?: string;
  nameAliases:      NameAlias[];

  // Contact
  email?:           string;           // optional (phone-only supported)
  phone:            string;           // required — E.164 format
  country:          string;           // ISO 3166-1 alpha-2
  countryDialCode:  string;

  // Profile
  headline:         string;
  bio:              string;
  profilePhoto?:    MediaAsset;
  coverPhoto?:      MediaAsset;
  profileUrl:       string;           // myprofile.com/@username — consumer platform domain, §50.8

  // Verification & Trust
  verificationStatus: VerificationStatus;
  trustScore:       TrustScore;
  badges:           Badge[];
  identityLayers:   IdentityLayer[];

  // Professional
  skills:           Skill[];
  tools:            Tool[];           // auto-populated from activities
  professions:      Profession[];
  industries:       Industry[];

  // Settings
  visibility:       VisibilitySettings;
  notifications:    NotificationSettings;
  language:         LanguageSettings;
  plan:             'free' | 'professional' | 'enterprise';

  // System
  createdAt:        Date;
  updatedAt:        Date;
  lastActive:       Date;
  consentLog:       ConsentRecord[];
  deviceTrust:      DeviceTrustRecord[];
}

type NameAliasType = 'legal' | 'professional' | 'native' | 'married' | 'former' | 'stage' | 'religious';

interface NameAlias {
  type: NameAliasType;
  value: string;
  language?: string;
}

// §50.9 Professional Growth Intelligence — Career Stage is separate from accountType/pathway
// on purpose: a student and a senior executive are both 'individual' accounts on the same
// pathway type; Career Stage is what evolves, never the account itself.
type CareerStage =
  | 'learner' | 'student' | 'undergraduate' | 'apprentice' | 'technical_vocational_learner'
  | 'emerging_professional' | 'fresh_graduate' | 'job_seeker' | 'intern'
  | 'national_service_participant' | 'professional' | 'freelancer' | 'entrepreneur'
  | 'researcher' | 'consultant' | 'skilled_tradesperson' | 'career_transition'
  | 'executive' | 'mentor' | 'retired_professional';
```

### 4.2 Organisation Schema

```typescript
interface Organisation {
  opid:             string;           // ORG-XXXXXX — immutable
  username:         string;           // @handle — globally unique
  orgType:          OrgType;
  legalName:        string;
  displayName:      string;

  // Contact
  officialEmail:    string;
  officialPhone:    string;
  country:          string;
  website?:         string;

  // Media
  logo?:            MediaAsset;
  coverBanner?:     MediaAsset;

  // Verification
  verificationStatus: VerificationStatus;
  trustScore:       TrustScore;
  badges:           Badge[];

  // People
  staff:            OrgStaffRecord[];
  adminRoles:       OrgAdminAssignment[]; // max 20 admins; records typed against OrgAdminRoleV3
                                           // (§42.1) — the superset of the base OrgAdminRole union
                                           // below, which adds the general 'admin' tier introduced
                                           // in §42.0/42.1, so this field and §42's operational
                                           // assignment records agree; §4.2's base OrgAdminRole
                                           // union is retained below for reference only.

  // VXI
  verificationSchemas: VerificationSchema[];
  credentialDatasets:  CredentialDataset[];
  verificationQueue:   VerificationQueueItem[];

  // Registration & Identity (extends §4.2)
  tradingName?:              string;
  registrationNumber?:       string;
  taxIdentificationNumber?:  string;
  headquartersAddress?:      string;   // distinct from `country` above
  departments?:              string[];
  branches?:                 { name: string; location: string }[];
  recruiterStatus?:          'active' | 'inactive';
  verificationAuthorityStatus?: boolean;  // true only if this org is itself an approved verifying body, per §16

  // Direct Connect (§39.13) — OFF by default, opt-in only
  openToDirectPitches?:      boolean;
  directPitchPreferences?:   DirectPitchPreferences;

  // System
  createdAt:        Date;
  plan:             'free' | 'professional' | 'enterprise';
}

type OrgType =
  | 'company' | 'startup' | 'ngo' | 'university' | 'school'
  | 'government_agency' | 'hospital' | 'cooperative' | 'guild'
  | 'trade_association' | 'certification_authority' | 'research_centre'
  | 'mda' | 'regulatory_body' | 'association' | 'licensing_board';

type OrgAdminRole =
  | 'super_admin' | 'verification_officer' | 'hr_manager'
  | 'credential_manager' | 'moderator' | 'compliance_officer';

// OrgStaffRecord: this type is referenced above (staff: OrgStaffRecord[]) and defined here.
// It is distinct from OrgAdminRole: this covers ordinary affiliations (employment, membership,
// enrolment); OrgAdminRole (§42) covers administrative/back-office access and may only be
// granted to someone who already holds an 'active' OrgStaffRecord here (§42.3 Rule 6).
interface OrgStaffRecord {
  userId:          string;              // PID of the affiliated individual
  orgId:           string;              // OPID
  affiliationType: 'employee' | 'faculty' | 'student' | 'alumni' | 'volunteer'
                   | 'contractor' | 'consultant' | 'board_member' | 'intern' | 'member';
  position?:       string;
  department?:     string;
  startDate:       Date;
  endDate?:        Date;
  officeLocation?: string;
  supervisorId?:   string;              // PID of supervising OrgStaffRecord holder, if any
  status:          'invited' | 'active' | 'ended' | 'declined';
  respondedAt?:    Date;
  auditTrail:      SecurityAuditEntry[]; // ties into §37.7, same pattern as OrgAdminAssignment (§42.1)
}

// PendingStaffInvite: covers the not-yet-registered path. Extends the existing onboarding
// identity-matching logic (§8) rather than adding a new invitation system.
interface PendingStaffInvite {
  orgId: string; name: string; email: string; position?: string; department?: string;
  status: 'pending_profile_registration';
  // On matching-email registration, §8's existing identity-matching auto-links this to a new
  // OrgStaffRecord — no re-entry of data required by the organisation.
}
```

```typescript
interface Activity {

  includeInPortfolio: boolean;        // default: true
  addToTrustEvidence: boolean;
  verificationStatus: VerificationStatus;

  // Visibility

  // Social

  comments:         Comment[];
  tags:             SelfTagRequest[];

  contentHash:      string;

}

type ActivityType =
  | 'work_log'
  | 'project'
  | 'research'
  | 'publication'
  | 'case_study'
  | 'milestone'
  | 'professional_achievement'
  | 'field_report'
  | 'learning_achievement'
  | 'certification_earned'
  | 'license_obtained'
  | 'portfolio_addition'
  | 'institutional_recognition'
  | 'industry_contribution';
```

### 4.4 Verification Schema

```typescript
interface VerificationRequest {
  id:               string;
  userId:           string;           // requestor PID
  type:             VerificationType;
  status:           VerificationStatus;

  // Institution
  institutionId:    string;
  institutionName:  string;
  schemaId:         string;           // institution-defined schema

  // Identifiers (UVIF — Universal Verification Identifier Framework)
  identifiers:      VerificationIdentifier[];

  // Documents
  uploads:          CredentialDocument[];

  // Timeline
  submittedAt:      Date;
  receivedAt?:      Date;
  reviewedAt?:      Date;
  approvedAt?:      Date;
  expiresAt?:       Date;

  // Audit
  auditLog:         AuditEntry[];
  confidenceScore:  number;           // 0–100
  matchResult:      MatchResult;

  // Outcome
  trustWeightAdded: number;
  badgeIssued?:     Badge;
  credentialHash:   string;
  verificationQR:   string;          // public verification URL
}

type VerificationType =
  | 'identity'
  | 'academic_affiliation'    // NEW — confirms attendance/enrolment ONLY ("this person studied here").
                               // Must never be displayed or implied as a degree, grade, or graduation claim.
  | 'academic'                 // Full credential confirmation — a specific degree/certificate was awarded.
                               // Distinct badge, distinct trigger (§14.2) — never inferred from the affiliation type above.
  | 'employment_affiliation'  // NEW — confirms someone worked there ONLY, not role/title/dates unless
                               // those are separately confirmed under 'employment' below.
  | 'employment'
  | 'professional_licence' | 'trade_competence' | 'certification'
  | 'membership_affiliation'  // NEW — confirms membership in a professional body/guild, distinct from
                               // that body separately certifying a credential (§16.2 institution-defined schema).
  | 'custom';

// HARD RULE (extends §16 VXI): one verification type must never automatically imply another.
// If an institution's schema (§16.2) only confirms 'academic_affiliation', PROFILE must never
// display, badge, or Trust-Score-weight that as if 'academic' (degree) had been confirmed.
// Precision here is what makes every other verified badge in this document meaningful.

type VerificationStatus =
  | 'draft' | 'submitted' | 'received' | 'institution_notified'
  | 'under_review' | 'approved' | 'verified' | 'active'
  | 'rejected' | 'appeal_available' | 'escalated'
  | 'expired' | 'renewal_required' | 'revoked' | 'suspended';

interface VerificationIdentifier {
  label:    string;    // "Student Number" / "Employee ID" / "Licence Number"
  value:    string;
  type:     string;    // from UVIF taxonomy
  required: boolean;
}
```

### 4.5 Trust Score Schema

```typescript
interface TrustScore {
  total:          number;             // 0–1000
  tier:           TrustTier;
  confidenceLevel: 'low' | 'moderate' | 'high' | 'institutional_grade';

  dimensions: {
    identityTrust:      number;
    academicTrust:      number;
    professionalTrust:  number;
    employmentTrust:    number;
    marketplaceTrust:   number;
    organisationalTrust: number;
    recruitmentTrust:   number;
    contributionTrust:  number;
  };

  signals:        TrustSignal[];      // every increment logged with source
  history:        TrustHistoryEntry[];
  formulaVersion: string;             // versioned for dispute reconstruction
  lastCalculated: Date;
  explainerUrl:   string;             // "How this is calculated"
}

interface TrustSignal {
  type:     string;
  weight:   number;
  source:   string;                   // e.g. "GlobalTech Inc (Employment)"
  date:     Date;
  verified: boolean;
}

type TrustTier = 'emerging' | 'established' | 'trusted' | 'expert' | 'master';

// TRUST_WEIGHTS: canonical definition lives at §13.2 (Trust Signal Weights) — not restated
// here to avoid two copies of the same values silently drifting apart if one is ever tuned.
type TrustWeightKey = keyof typeof TRUST_WEIGHTS;   // §13.2
```

```typescript
interface CVDocument {
  id:             string;
  userId:         string;
  pathway:        'professional' | 'competence' | 'organisation';

  sections: {
    summary:          CVSection<SummaryContent>;
    workExperience:   CVSection<WorkEntry[]>;
    education:        CVSection<EducationEntry[]>;
    skills:           CVSection<SkillEntry[]>;
    tools:            CVSection<ToolEntry[]>;

    portfolio:        CVSection<PortfolioItem[]>;
    clientReviews:    CVSection<ReviewEntry[]>;
  };

  // Display
  visibility:     SectionVisibility;
  livePreview:    boolean;
  pdfReady:       boolean;            // Premium
  qrCode:         string;
  recruiterView:  boolean;
  atsCompatible:  boolean;

  // Auto-triggers
  autoUpdateOn:   AutoUpdateTrigger[];
  lastUpdated:    Date;
}

interface CVSection<T> {
  visible:        boolean;
  order:          number;
  data:           T;
  verifiedItems:  boolean;
  lastAutoUpdate: Date;
}
```

## 5. ECOSYSTEM LOOP & FEATURE LINKAGE MAP

### 5.1 Data Dependency Chain

Every arrow below exists as a **real data dependency** — a shared evidence ID or reference. If Activity cannot mechanically feed Portfolio (through a shared evidence ID), the chain is broken regardless of how the modules are described in copy.

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    PROFILE ECOSYSTEM LOOP                           │
│                                                                     │
│  ┌──────────┐    ┌───────────┐    ┌──────────────┐                 │
│  │ ACTIVITY │───▶│ PORTFOLIO │───▶│  DISCOVERY   │                 │
│  └──────────┘    └───────────┘    └──────────────┘                 │
│       │                                   │                         │
│       │ (evidence_id)                     ▼                         │
│       ▼                           ┌──────────────┐                 │
│  ┌──────────────┐                 │ OPPORTUNITY  │                 │
│  │  TRUST SCORE │◀────────────────└──────────────┘                 │
│  └──────────────┘                                                   │
│       │                                                             │
│       ▼                                                             │
│  ┌──────────────┐    ┌──────────────────┐                          │
│  │ VERIFICATION │───▶│ RECRUITER        │                          │
│  └──────────────┘    │ CONFIDENCE       │                          │
│                       └──────────────────┘                          │
│                                   │                                 │
│                                   ▼                                 │
│                           ┌──────────────┐                          │
│                           │    HIRING    │                          │
│                           └──────────────┘                          │
│                                   │                                 │
│                                   ▼                                 │
│                           ┌──────────────┐                          │
│                           │ WORK EVIDENCE│                          │
│                           └──────────────┘                          │
│                                   │                                 │
│                                   └──────────────▶ [ACTIVITY] ◀── ┘│
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 5.2 Feature-to-Feature Linkage Table

| From Feature | Feeds Into | Shared Key | Trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| Activity (type, skills, tools) | CV / Portfolio | `activity.id → cvSection.sourceIds[]` | `includeInCV = true` |
| Activity (tools tagged) | ToolStack | `tool.id → user.tools[]` | Auto — every tag |
| Activity (org mentioned) | Org Notification | `orgId → notification queue` | Auto — on save |
| Activity (media upload) | Copyright Vault | `copyrightId #PRF-YYYY-XXXX` | Auto — on upload |
| Activity (published) | Trust Evidence Pool | `activity.id → trustEvidence[]` | User toggle |
| Trust Evidence | Verification Request | `evidenceId → verificationRequest.evidenceRefs[]` | User action |
| Verification (approved) | Trust Score | `verification.trustWeight → trustScore.signals[]` | Auto — on approval |
| Trust Score | Discovery Ranking | `trustScore.total → discoveryRank` | Real-time |
| Trust Score | CV / Portfolio | `trustScore → cvSection.trustPanel` | Real-time |
| Discovery | Opportunity Match | `discoveryRank + skills → opportunityMatch[]` | Scheduled |
| Opportunity (hired) | Work Evidence | `hiring.record → activity (new)` | Auto-prompt |
| Work Evidence | Activity | `workEvidence.id → activity.sourceRef` | Completes loop |
| Verification (badge issued) | Profile Badges | `badge.id → user.badges[]` | Auto |
| Verification (badge issued) | CV Badges | `badge.id → cvSection.certifications[].badge` | Auto |
| Collaborator Tag (approved) | Tagged user's CV | `tagApproval → activity on tagger's CV` | On approval |
| Client Review (verified) | Trust Score | `review.trustWeight → trustScore.signals[]` | Auto |
| Client Review (verified) | CV Reviews section | `review.id → cvSection.clientReviews[]` | Auto |
| Course Completed | CV Certifications | `course.completionId → cvSection.certifications[]` | Auto |
| Course Completed | Trust Score | `+8 → trustScore` | Auto |
| Org Follow | Org Notification | `follow.event → org.followerAlert` | Auto |
| Portfolio | Discovery Index | `portfolio.strength → discoveryScore.portfolioWeight` | On publish |

### 5.3 Auto-Update Triggers (CV / Portfolio)

```typescript
const CV_AUTO_UPDATE_TRIGGERS: AutoUpdateTrigger[] = [
  { event: 'activity.published',        section: 'workExperience', delay: 0 },
  { event: 'verification.approved',     section: 'all_relevant',   delay: 0 },
  { event: 'client_review.posted',      section: 'clientReviews',  delay: 0 },
  { event: 'certification.completed',   section: 'certifications', delay: 0 },
  { event: 'skill.tag.approved',        section: 'skills',         delay: 0 },
  { event: 'collaboration.approved',    section: 'collaborations', delay: 0 },
  { event: 'trust_score.changed',       section: 'trustPanel',     delay: 0 },
  { event: 'org.employment.verified',   section: 'workExperience', delay: 0 },
];
// All triggers: < 2 second update cycle — non-negotiable
```

## 6. HERO GATEWAY & LANDING EXPERIENCE

### 6.1 Philosophy

The Hero is the global gateway into PROFILE — not an information page, not a marketing
page, not a documentation page. Its sole purpose is to introduce PROFILE, establish trust,
and route the visitor into the correct next step in seconds: *Identity → Evidence →
Verification → Trust → Opportunity → Economic Growth.* Every other piece of information
about PROFILE lives one click away, under Explore PROFILE (§6.8) — never bolted onto the
Hero itself.

### 6.2 Logo System

```
USE ONLY: Approved PROFILE SVG asset — never a PNG substitute where SVG is available
DO NOT:   Recreate · Redesign · Replace · Raster-ise

The logo is the Hero's visual centerpiece — fully integrated and perfectly centered on
desktop, tablet, and mobile alike, never a small corner mark on this page specifically
(it remains a small header mark on every other page, per §12 Navigation Architecture).

Variants:        Dark mode · Light mode
Rendering:       SVG · Retina-ready · Responsive scaling, crisp at every breakpoint
Animation:       LOGO_ANIMATION_SEQUENCE (see 6.3)
```

### 6.3 Logo Animation Sequence

```typescript
// Duration: 2.5s–3.5s | Premium motion | No aggressive looping
const LOGO_ANIMATION: AnimationPhase[] = [
  { phase: 1, name: 'SoftGlow',        duration: 400, easing: 'easeOut' },
  { phase: 2, name: 'StructureDraw',   duration: 700, easing: 'easeInOut' },
  { phase: 3, name: 'IdentityReveal',  duration: 500, easing: 'easeOut' },
  { phase: 4, name: 'VerificationPulse', duration: 400, easing: 'easeInOut' },
  { phase: 5, name: 'NetworkExpand',   duration: 600, easing: 'easeOut' },
];
```

### 6.4 Hero Background — Ecosystem Silhouettes, Never Floating Cards

```typescript
// No floating information cards on the Hero. Instead: a subtle animated background of
// low-opacity SVG silhouettes and icons representing what PROFILE actually does —
// legible as shapes, never as readable text.
const HERO_VISUAL_LAYERS = [
  'professional_profile_icons_and_silhouettes',
  'human_connection_and_collaboration_symbols',
  'trust_shields_and_verification_badges',
  'academic_caps_and_certificates',
  'skills_and_tools_iconography',
  'briefcases_and_research_symbols',
  'buildings_universities_government_and_company_icons',
  'portfolio_and_cv_document_symbols',
  'opportunity_marketplace_and_collaboration_icons',
  'messaging_and_ai_symbols',
  'global_network_nodes_slow_parallax',
];

const HERO_BACKGROUND_RULES = {
  fade: 'gentle', movement: 'slow', neverDistracts: true, neverOverlapsLogo: true,
  opacity: '5%–10% MAX', supportsLightAndDarkMode: true,
  implementation: 'SVG + CSS animation preferred over video',
};

// HARD RULE: the only visible focal points on the Hero are —
// 1. PROFILE Logo (centered)   2. Primary Headline   3. Short Mission Statement
// 4. Create Your PROFILE CTA  5. Sign In CTA          6. Explore PROFILE CTA
// No additional sections appear on the Hero itself — see §6.8 for where everything else lives.
```

### 6.5 Hero Content

```tsx
<HeroHeadline>
  Build Your Identity. Verify Your Value. Unlock Opportunity.
</HeroHeadline>

<HeroMission>
  {/* Concise — never a long paragraph */}
  PROFILE is a Professional Identity Platform powered by PROFILE OS — connecting
  identity, evidence, verification, trust, and opportunity in one place.
</HeroMission>

<CTAGroup>
  <PrimaryButton label="Create Your PROFILE" onClick={handleCreateProfile} />
  <SecondaryButton label="Sign In" onClick={handleSignIn} />
  {/* NEW — third primary action, replaces "scroll to learn more" */}
  <TertiaryButton label="Explore PROFILE" onClick={() => router.push('/explore')} />
</CTAGroup>
```

### 6.6 Create Profile & Sign In Routing Logic

```typescript
// REQUIRED FLOW — zero exceptions
function handleCreateProfile(authState: AuthState) {
  if (!authState.isAuthenticated) { router.push('/auth?mode=signup'); return; }
  if (authState.isAuthenticated && !authState.onboardingComplete) { router.push('/onboarding'); return; }
  if (authState.isAuthenticated && authState.onboardingComplete) { router.push('/home'); return; }
}

function handleSignIn() {
  // Selecting Sign In routes IMMEDIATELY to authentication — no intermediate screen.
  router.push('/auth');
}

// GUARDS
// Users must NEVER: skip authentication · skip onboarding · skip verification
// NEVER route back to Hero after clicking Create Profile
// NEVER allow loading loops or page refresh loops
// ALWAYS show: loading state on click · progress indicator · state persistence
// Upon successful authentication, return the user to their last valid location, or /home.
```

### 6.7 Universal Navigation — No Page May Trap the User

```typescript
// Applies to every public-facing page, not just the Hero.
const UNIVERSAL_NAV_REQUIREMENTS = ['back_button', 'previous_button', 'home_link'];
```

Everything that is not the Hero's six focal points (§6.4) lives here, reached only via the
Explore PROFILE CTA — never surfaced as scroll-triggered Hero content. This is also where
the storytelling content originally drafted for the Hero now lives, unchanged in substance,
relocated in role:

```typescript
];

{ headline: 'VISION', body: 'A world where every person and institution can prove identity, demonstrate work, and access opportunities through evidence and trust.',
  features: ['Global Reach', 'Trusted Identity', 'Verified Work', 'Opportunity Access', 'Economic Growth'] }

// MISSION — orbiting profile elements
{ headline: 'MISSION', body: 'Build the world\'s most trusted professional identity infrastructure where work becomes credibility, credibility becomes opportunity, and opportunity becomes growth.',
  orbitItems: ['identity', 'verification', 'portfolio', 'institutions', 'opportunities', 'collaboration'] }

// PROBLEMS WE SOLVE — each problem animates → transforms → solution
```

Users always return to the Hero from Explore PROFILE via Back, Previous, or Home

## 7. AUTHENTICATION SYSTEM

### 7.1 Sign-In Screen

```tsx
// Full-screen: animated background pattern + PROFILE logo centred
// Tagline: "Where Work Becomes Credibility."

<SignInScreen>
  {/* Unified Smart Input — auto-detects type */}
  <SmartInput
    label="Email, @Username or Phone Number"
    detect={{
      email:    /^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$/,
      username: /^@/,
      phone:    'E.164_detection_all_country_codes',
    }}
  />

  {/* Password */}
  <PasswordField
    showHideToggle={true}
    strengthIndicator={true}
    inlineValidation={true}
  />

  {/* Social Auth — official branding + icons required */}
  <SocialAuthButtons>
    <GoogleAuth    brand="official_google_branding" />
    <MicrosoftAuth brand="official_microsoft_branding" />
    <AppleAuth     brand="official_apple_branding" />
  </SocialAuthButtons>

  {/* Controls */}
  <RememberMeToggle />
  <ForgotPasswordLink route="/auth/recover" />
</SignInScreen>
```

```typescript
    escalation: 'captcha → lockout → manual_review',
  },
  csrf: {
    tokenOnAllRequests: true,
  },
  rateLimit: {
    authEndpoints: '10 requests per minute per IP',
  },
  deviceFingerprint: true,
};
```

## 8. REGISTRATION & ONBOARDING SYSTEM

### 8.1 Governing Principle

Registration must be **fast, global, and frictionless** for every account type — get users
inside PROFILE in five steps, none of them heavy. Everything else is completed inside the
platform, in Settings, the Trust Center, or the CV/Portfolio Engine.

### 8.2 Absolute Prohibition During Sign-Up

```
ZERO EXCEPTIONS:
✗ NO credential uploads   ✗ NO certificates   ✗ NO employment records
✗ NO matriculation numbers   ✗ NO licenses · Staff IDs · Verification documents
All of this belongs EXCLUSIVELY inside the Trust Center, post-registration.
```

### 8.3 Step 1 — Choose Your Account Type

Prompt: *"Choose your account type to get started."*

```typescript
type IndividualCategory = 'professional' | 'competence' | 'hybrid';

const INDIVIDUAL_CATEGORIES: Record<IndividualCategory, { description: string; examples: string[] }> = {
  professional: {
    description: 'Regulated, licensed, degree-based professions',
    examples: ['Doctor', 'Lawyer', 'Engineer', 'Architect', 'Pharmacist', 'Accountant', 'Nurse', 'Surveyor', 'Scientist', 'Professor'],
  },
  competence: {
    description: 'Skill-based, trade-based, vocational, freelance, artisan, and creative professions',
    examples: ['Welder', 'Photographer', 'Chef', 'Electrician', 'Carpenter', 'Tailor', 'Graphic Designer', 'Mechanic', 'Hair Stylist', 'Videographer'],
  },
  hybrid: {
    description: 'Academic qualifications combined with professional competencies',
    examples: ['Engineer & Fabricator', 'Doctor & Entrepreneur', 'Architect & Builder', 'Lecturer & Consultant'],
  },
};
// All three individual categories: full access to CV, Portfolio, Credentials, Trust
// Center, Verification, Professional Recommendations, Opportunity Hub, Analytics (§8.5).

// careerStage (§50.9.1) is defined on the User schema and
// consumed throughout PGI (§50.9.1–50.9.9) but previously had no onboarding capture
// point. Added here as a same-screen follow-on question after IndividualCategory,
// per the original PGI patch instruction — not a new screen or step.
const CAREER_STAGE_PROMPT = 'Where are you in your career right now?';
const CAREER_STAGE_OPTIONS: CareerStage[] = [
  'learner', 'student', 'undergraduate', 'apprentice', 'technical_vocational_learner',
  'emerging_professional', 'fresh_graduate', 'job_seeker', 'intern',
  'national_service_participant', 'professional', 'freelancer', 'entrepreneur',
  'researcher', 'consultant', 'skilled_tradesperson', 'career_transition',
  'executive', 'mentor', 'retired_professional',
];
// Optional at signup — skippable, defaults to unset (consistent with §50.9.10's backward-
// compatibility rule) and editable anytime from Settings or the Profile page thereafter.

const ORGANISATION_TYPES = [
  'Company', 'Startup', 'University', 'Polytechnic', 'College', 'School',
  'Government Ministry', 'Government Agency', 'Regulatory Authority', 'Licensing Board',
  'Professional Council', 'Certification Authority', 'Hospital', 'NGO', 'Foundation',
  'Research Institute', 'Trade Association', 'Guild', 'Cooperative', 'Recruitment Agency',
  'Consulting Firm', 'Any recognised legal entity',
];
// Organisation accounts: Portfolio, Verification Schemas, Recruitment, Opportunity Hub,
// Institution Dashboard, Analytics, Marketplace — NEVER a CV module, structurally, not
// just visually hidden (restates §15.2, §49.5's hard rule).
```

### 8.4 Account-Type Routing Matrix & Hub Differentiation

Selecting an account type and organisation category does not just gate CV vs. Portfolio —
it routes the account to the specific operational hub that type actually needs. Hubs are
never one-size-fits-all for every organisation; a university and a startup do not need the
same dashboard emphasis, even though both are "Organisation" accounts.

```typescript
type OrgHubProfile = 'institutional_hub' | 'business_hub' | 'regulatory_console' | 'community_hub';

const ORG_TYPE_TO_HUB: Record<string, OrgHubProfile> = {
  University: 'institutional_hub', Polytechnic: 'institutional_hub', College: 'institutional_hub',
  School: 'institutional_hub', 'Research Institute': 'institutional_hub', 'Certification Authority': 'institutional_hub',
  Company: 'business_hub', Startup: 'business_hub', 'Recruitment Agency': 'business_hub', 'Consulting Firm': 'business_hub', Hospital: 'business_hub',
  'Government Ministry': 'regulatory_console', 'Government Agency': 'regulatory_console', 'Regulatory Authority': 'regulatory_console', 'Licensing Board': 'regulatory_console', 'Professional Council': 'regulatory_console',
  NGO: 'community_hub', Foundation: 'community_hub', 'Trade Association': 'community_hub', Guild: 'community_hub', Cooperative: 'community_hub',
};

const HUB_EMPHASIS: Record<OrgHubProfile, string[]> = {
  institutional_hub: ['bulk_credential_verification_§16.3', 'institutional_schema_builder_§16.2', 'alumni_and_graduate_verification', 'academic_opportunity_engine_§18.4'],
  business_hub: ['recruitment_dashboard_§18.3', 'hiring_pipeline', 'organisation_analytics_§23', 'marketplace_storefront_§19'],
  regulatory_console: ['professional_licence_verification_§16', 'compliance_reporting_§27.4', 'credential_authority_status_§21_IOTR', 'audit_log_access_§37.7'],
  community_hub: ['membership_verification', 'guild_and_association_directories', 'peer_verification_§34_Learning_and_Communities', 'community_opportunity_board'],
};
// RULE: every OrganisationType maps to exactly one OrgHubProfile — no organisation account
// lands on a generic, undifferentiated dashboard. New organisation types added later must
// be assigned a hub profile before they can register, not left to default silently.
```

### 8.4.1 No Anonymous Organisation Creation (extends §8.3/§8.4, §4.2)

```
RULE — ORGANISATION CREATOR MUST ALREADY HOLD A VERIFIED INDIVIDUAL PROFILE ACCOUNT
The account initiating Organisation registration (§8.3) must be signed in as a verified
Individual PROFILE account, identified by @username or registered email — organisation
accounts are never created anonymously or standalone. That individual is automatically:
  1. Set as the organisation's initial super_admin (OrgAdminRole, §42.1), and
  2. Given the organisation's first OrgStaffRecord (§4.2) — affiliationType 'employee' or
     the closest equivalent for the org type, status 'active' — ahead of inviting anyone else.
This does not change the org registration screens themselves; it constrains who may reach
them (must already be authenticated as an Individual) and what is created automatically
on submission.
```

### 8.5 Step 2 — Account Information & Email Verification

```typescript
// INDIVIDUAL
const INDIVIDUAL_INFO_FIELDS = ['legalName', 'preferredDisplayName_optional', 'country', 'dateOfBirth', 'email', 'password', 'confirmPassword'];

// Age validation — minimum 16, never displayed publicly (§9.3 Layer 1 private field)
interface AgeGateResult { allowed: boolean; reason?: 'under_minimum_age'; }
function validateAge(dob: Date, country: string): AgeGateResult {
  const minAge = minimumAgeForCountry(country);   // §16.6 country adapter — 16 or higher
  return calculatedAge(dob) >= minAge ? { allowed: true } : { allowed: false, reason: 'under_minimum_age' };
}
// If blocked: the submitting email enters a temporary restricted list to prevent repeated
// automated attempts — NOT a permanent punitive blacklist. A clear explanation and an
// appeal process are shown, since date-of-birth entry errors happen and false positives
// must be correctable (consistent with §50.1's Reputation Recovery principle).

// ORGANISATION
const ORG_INFO_FIELDS = ['registeredLegalName', 'tradingName_optional', 'organisationCategory', 'country', 'registrationNumber_optional', 'officialEmail', 'password', 'confirmPassword'];

// EMAIL VERIFICATION — both account types
interface EmailVerificationToken {
  code: string;              // six digits
  validitySeconds: 60;
  resendSupported: true;
  maxResendAttempts: number; // configurable, rate-limited per §37.2
}
```

### 8.6 Step 3 — Consent (Two Grouped Agreements)

```typescript
const CONSENT_GROUPS = [
  {
    id: 'identity_and_conduct',
    label: 'Identity & Professional Conduct',
    bundles: ['identity_authenticity_agreement', 'verification_routing_consent', 'professional_conduct_standards'],
  },
  {
    id: 'privacy_and_data',
    label: 'Privacy & Data',
    bundles: ['privacy_policy', 'data_processing_agreement'],
  },
];
// Exactly two checkboxes — each title links to its full bundled document (§46). Both
// require active consent before continuing; all consents remain versioned, timestamped,
// IP-logged, legally auditable, and revocable, per §27.4's existing compliance framework.
```

Prompt: *"Select all that apply."*

```typescript
const ONBOARDING_GOALS = [
  'Find employment', 'Hire talent', 'Showcase my work', 'Verify credentials',

  'Showcase my research', 'Win new clients',

// Fourteen options, multi-select. Personalises onboarding and initial dashboard without

```

```typescript
const PROFILE_SETUP_FIELDS = ['profilePicture', 'coverPicture', 'username', 'professionalHeadline'];
// Cover picture is always visible on the account when others visit the page — not a
// hidden/optional decorative element once set.

const GENERATION_MODES = {
  ai_auto: 'Instant draft, fully editable',
  manual:  'Blank slate, guided sections',
  hybrid:  'AI draft + manual customisation',
};
// Username: live availability check. Directories (professions/trades/competencies/

```

### 8.8.1 Journey Setup — Strategic Activity Recording From Day One (new onboarding
sub-step, extends §8.7/§8.8, before §8.9 routing)

```typescript
// Triggered once, immediately after Step 5 (§8.8), before first routing to /home.
// Branches on the careerStage already selected in Step 1 (§8.3) — asks nothing that
// wasn't already implied by that choice. Full rationale and delivery mechanism: §50.9.11.

const ACADEMIC_JOURNEY_SETUP = {
  trigger: "careerStage in ['learner','student','undergraduate','technical_vocational_learner']",
  prompt: "What stage are you starting at?",
  options: [
    'Foundation / Pre-university', 'Year 1 (Bachelor\u2019s / Poly / Diploma)', 'Year 2', 'Year 3',
    'Final Year', 'Master\u2019s — Year 1', 'Master\u2019s — Final Year',
    'PhD — Early Stage', 'PhD — Final Stage', 'Postgraduate Diploma',
  ],
  alsoAsks: ['fieldOfStudy', 'institution (optional — links via §16 enrolment affiliation if registered)'],
  outcome: 'Sets journeyStage, used only to select which strategic journey checklist (§50.9.11) is shown first.',
};

  trigger: "pathway === 'competence' AND no institution affiliation selected",
  prompt: "Where are you in building your craft?",
  options: ['Just starting out', 'Building a body of work', 'Already taking on clients or selling'],
  alsoAsks: ['craftOrTradeArea'],
  offersImmediately: 'a draft Marketplace Store listing (§19) — set up now, published when ready, never forced live.',
  outcome: 'Sets journeyStage. This branch carries identical seriousness and visibility as the
            academic path — it is a first-class onboarding branch, not a fallback shown only
            to people who skip the academic option.',
};
```

### 8.9 Completion & Routing

```typescript
function completeRegistration(accountType: AccountContextV3) {
  createAccount(); initializeSchemas(); provisionModules(accountType);  // §8.4 hub routing applied here
  generateProfileId();
  return accountType.accountType === 'individual' ? '/home' : `/org-dashboard?hub=${resolveHub(accountType)}`;
}
```

```typescript
// ROUTING MATRIX — no user ever sees a page or control that doesn't apply to their account
const INDIVIDUAL_DEFAULT_MODULES = ['my_profile', 'cv', 'portfolio', 'trust_center', 'opportunity_hub', 'marketplace', 'messaging', 'analytics', 'settings'];
const ORGANISATION_DEFAULT_MODULES = ['organisation_profile', 'portfolio', 'verification_centre', 'recruitment_dashboard', 'opportunity_hub', 'marketplace', 'messaging', 'analytics', 'settings'];
// Organisation accounts additionally receive their §8.4 hub-specific emphasis modules.
```

## 9. GLOBAL IDENTITY RESOLUTION & PROFILE IDENTITY GRAPH

### 9.1 Core Principle

**Names are not identities. Evidence is identity.**

**Connection model (clarification, no new mechanism):** PROFILE's
connections are a **Trust Graph**, not a follow/friend social graph. A connection's visible
weight is already governed by verified work, credentials, endorsements, and collaboration
(§13.2 Trust Signal Weights) rather than a raw connect/follow action — this is a naming
clarification of existing behavior defined at §13, not a schema or ranking change.

### 9.2 Profile Identity ID (PID)

```typescript
const PID_FORMAT = 'PRF-9X7K-24AB-MQ8D';   // or: P.2025.84F3D8B9
// Properties:
// - Immutable: never changes
// - Never reused: ever
// - Never public: internal anchor only
// - Used for: verification · credential issuance · trust scoring · audit logs

const OPID_FORMAT = 'ORG-XXXXXX';           // Organisation PID
```

### 9.3 Identity Layers (All 5 per Account)

```typescript
const IDENTITY_LAYERS = {
  layer1_core: {
    fields: ['fullLegalName', 'dateOfBirth (private)', 'country', 'identityDocuments', 'verificationStatus'],
  },
  layer2_contact: {
    fields: ['email', 'phone', 'recoveryMethods'],
    note: 'Can change over time',
  },
  layer3_professional: {
    fields: ['profession', 'skills', 'credentials', 'education', 'licenses', 'employment'],
  },
  layer4_institutional: {
    sources: ['Universities', 'Employers', 'Regulators', 'Certification Bodies'],
  },
  layer5_contribution: {
    sources: ['Projects', 'Work', 'Research', 'Services', 'Marketplace Activity', 'Publications'],
  },
};
```

### 9.4 Name Collision Resolution

```typescript
// NEVER display only names. Always display alongside:
interface UserDisplay {
  name:              string;
  headline:          string;    // "Civil Engineer"
  country:           string;    // "Canada"
  trustStatus:       string;    // "Verified Professional"
  username:          string;    // "@johnsmith.engineer"
}
// Example: "John Smith / Civil Engineer / Canada / Verified Professional / @johnsmith.engineer"
```

### 9.5 Anti-Impersonation Engine

```typescript
// Continuous scanning for:
const SCAN_TARGETS = [
  'fake_professionals', 'fake_institutions', 'fake_recruiters',
  'profile_similarity', 'credential_similarity', 'photo_similarity',
  'institution_claims', 'behavioural_patterns',
];
```

### 9.6 Credential Ownership Engine

```typescript
interface CredentialOwnership {
  credentialHash:       string;   // Cannot be copied
  ownerHash:            string;   // Cannot be reassigned
  institutionHash:      string;
  timestamp:            Date;
  verificationSignature: string;
  trustWeight:          number;
}
// RULE: Cannot be copied to another profile. Cannot be reassigned. Cannot be duplicated.
```

### 9.7 Private Professional Mode (extends §4.1 NameAlias, §4.3 Activity.visibility,
§12.2 Identity Verification — not a new verification tier, a display and disclosure rule)

```
The need: a person whose primary occupation cannot be publicly named — an intelligence,
security, diplomatic, or safety-sensitive professional, but equally a journalist, a
domestic-abuse survivor rebuilding a public career, or a performer working under a stage
name — should still be able to build a fully verified PROFILE around the rest of their
professional and creative life, without that life reading as suspicious, incomplete, or
less trustworthy for what it doesn't show.

This is a general privacy capability available to anyone, not a special account type —
narrow, name-a-use-case features age badly and leak exactly the information they're meant
to protect. The mechanism is entirely built from pieces this document already has a home
for; nothing new is verified, and nothing already verified becomes less verified.
```

```typescript
interface PrivateProfessionalModeSettings {
  publicDisplayIdentity: NameAliasType;   // defaults to 'legal'; may be set to 'professional',
                                            // 'stage', or any other existing alias type (§4.1) —
                                            // the legal name stays privately verified underneath
                                            // (§12.2 Identity Verification) whether or not it is
                                            // ever the name shown to the public
  legalNamePublicVisibility: 'public' | 'private';  // 'private' does not remove or weaken the
                                            // identity_verification Trust Score weight (§13.2) —
                                            // the Verified Identity badge (§14.2) still displays;
                                            // it just never discloses which legal name it verified
  employmentHistoryDisclosure: 'full' | 'partial' | 'undisclosed_period';
                                            // an 'undisclosed_period' entry is a normal Activity
                                            // record (§4.3) with visibility: 'private' — it exists
                                            // in the evidence base and can still be verified
                                            // through §42's Verified Affiliation flow if the
                                            // employer is willing, but is never rendered on the
                                            // public CV/Portfolio or Discovery (§17)
}

const NON_DISCRIMINATION_RULE = [
  'an_undisclosed_or_private_employment_period_is_never_treated_by_profile_health_(§50.9.3)_
   career_readiness_(§50.9.4)_or_any_completeness_indicator_as_a_gap_missing_information_or_
   red_flag',
  'trust_score_(§13)_is_computed_from_whatever_verified_evidence_exists_—_it_never_penalises_
   the_absence_of_evidence_a_person_has_chosen_not_to_disclose',
  'recruiters_and_discovery_(§17.1)_see_exactly_the_same_hard_rule_as_every_other_profile_—_
   ranking_by_verified_evidence_present_never_by_completeness_percentage',
];
// A private year on someone's timeline reads, correctly, as private — not as a lie, a gap,
// or a lower trust signal. This is what makes Private Professional Mode actually usable
// rather than a feature people are afraid to turn on.
```

---

## 10. NAVIGATION ARCHITECTURE

### 10.0 Philosophy

This is a structural UI/UX and information-architecture standard, not a new feature set.
Every module referenced below already exists elsewhere in this document — nothing here
introduces new business logic. Every screen must answer one question: *"What is the next
professional action the user should take?"* Navigation should make recording professional
evidence feel like a daily habit, not a chore.

```
No feature is removed. Overlapping systems merge into unified hubs. Mobile-first design,
optimised for one-handed, thumb-friendly interaction.
```

### 10.1 Mobile Bottom Navigation (5 Destinations, Center Primary Action)

```
DESIGN PRINCIPLE: the bottom navigation exposes the actions users perform every day —
not every feature the platform has. Once a user understands PROFILE, they rarely tap a
dedicated Discover tab; they instinctively use Search. Opportunity, by contrast, is one
of PROFILE's core promises (§1.4, §7's Identity → ... → Opportunity chain) — burying it
in a menu buries one of the platform's biggest value propositions. Discover therefore
moves into the persistent global search (§10.2); Opportunity takes its place in the
bottom navigation.
```

```typescript
const BOTTOM_TABS = [
  {
    id: 'home', icon: '🏠', label: 'Home', route: '/home',
    contains: ['dashboard', 'professional_activity_feed', 'trust_updates', 'recommended_opportunities', 'portfolio_highlights'],
  },
  {
    id: 'connections', icon: '🤝', label: 'Connections', route: '/connections',
    contains: ['professional_network', 'mentors', 'recruiters', 'teams', 'communities_shortcut', 'invitations'],
  },
  {
    id: 'create_activity', icon: '➕', label: 'Create Activity', route: '/record', isPrimaryAction: true,
    contains: [
      'create_professional_activity', 'upload_media', 'add_evidence', 'tag_skills', 'tag_tools',
      'tag_collaborators', 'tag_organisations', 'tag_institutions', 'add_to_cv', 'add_to_portfolio', 'save_draft',
    ],
  },
  {
    id: 'opportunity', icon: '💼', label: 'Opportunity', route: '/opportunities',
    contains: [
      'jobs', 'contracts', 'internships', 'scholarships', 'fellowships', 'grants',
      'volunteer_opportunities', 'collaborations', 'saved_opportunities', 'applications', 'recruiter_invitations',
    ],
  },
  {
    id: 'cv_portfolio', icon: '📄', label: { individual: 'CV', organisation: 'Portfolio' }, route: '/cv',
    contains: [
      'dynamic_cv_builder', 'portfolio', 'resume_templates', 'evidence_library',
      'version_history', 'sharing', 'download', 'permissions',
    ],
  },
];

// CREATE ACTIVITY — the center primary action, PROFILE's heartbeat (§11 Activity Engine)
const CREATE_ACTIVITY_BUTTON_RULES = {
  alwaysVisible: true, centered: true, visuallyEmphasised: true,
  floatsAboveNavBar: true, usesColor: 'var(--color-royal-blue)', // §3.1 — no separate palette
  idleMotion: 'subtle', hapticFeedback: 'where_available',
  neverHiddenByScrollOrResponsiveLayout: true,
};
// Every activity created here feeds the same Activity Engine (§11), Tagging Engine (§14
// legacy numbering / §4.3 Activity schema), and CV/Portfolio toggle already specified —
// this is a navigation entry point, not a second activity system.

const NAVIGATION_RATIONALE = {
  home: 'Check their professional feed',
  connections: 'Maintain relationships',
  create_activity: 'Contribute new work',
  opportunity: 'Look for opportunities',
  cv_portfolio: 'Present their professional identity',
};
// These five map directly onto Learning → Identity → Evidence → Verification → Trust →
// Opportunity → Growth → Economic Prosperity (§1.4, §50.9.0). Everything else supports
// these five core activities and lives in Search (§10.2) or the Hamburger Menu (§10.4).
```

### 10.2 Top App Bar (Mobile) — Discover Lives Here Now

```tsx
<TopAppBar>
  <Left>
    <UserAvatar opens="profile_drawer" />  {/* §10.3 — distinct from the hamburger, §10.4 */}
  </Left>
  <Centre>
    <UniversalSearch placeholder="Search people, jobs, organisations, skills, credentials..." />
    {/* Discover (§17 Smart Search) is now fully integrated here rather than occupying a
        bottom-nav slot. Indexes: people, students, professionals, organisations,
        universities, governments, companies, recruiters, communities, jobs, opportunities,
        skills, tools, services, marketplace, projects, research, publications,
        credentials, portfolios, activities — same ranking Hard Rule as §17.1: trust-based,
        never popularity-based. */}
  </Centre>
  <Right>
    <NotificationBell badgeCount={unreadCount} />
    <MessageIcon badgeCount={unreadMessages} />
    <HamburgerMenu opens="ecosystem_menu" />  {/* §10.4 */}
  </Right>
</TopAppBar>
// Collapses elegantly on scroll; Universal Search remains reachable at all times.
```

### 10.3 Profile Drawer (Avatar Menu) — Quick Personal Access Only

Opened by tapping the avatar — kept deliberately short; everything else lives in the
Hamburger Menu (§10.4) so the two never duplicate each other:

```typescript
const PROFILE_DRAWER_ITEMS = [
  { label: 'My Profile',   route: '/profile/@username' },
  { label: 'Edit Profile',  route: '/profile/edit' },
  { label: 'My CV', route: '/cv', showFor: 'individual' },       // structurally absent for organisations, §15.2/§49.5
  { label: 'My Portfolio', route: '/portfolio' },
  { divider: true },
  // only rendered when the signed-in individual holds at least one
  // active OrgAdminAssignment (§42.1) or OrgStaffRecord with dashboard access (§4.2):
  { label: 'Switch Workspace', action: 'openWorkspaceSwitcher',
    showsList: 'Personal PROFILE + every organisation where the user holds an active role',
    condition: 'has_active_OrgAdminAssignment_or_dashboard_eligible_OrgStaffRecord' },
  { divider: true },
  { label: 'Sign Out', action: 'signOut' },
];
// Workspace switching is a single-session context switch (§7.4 Session Management) — no
// second login and no second account are created; it is a UI lens over the same PID.
```

### 10.4 Hamburger Menu (Ecosystem-Wide, Infrequent Access)

The full set of secondary modules, adapted by role and verification status:

```typescript
const HAMBURGER_MENU_ITEMS = [
  { label: 'My Profile',            route: '/profile/@username' },
  { label: 'Trust Center',          route: '/trust' },
  { label: 'Verification Center',   route: '/trust/verification' },
  { label: 'Credential Hub',        route: '/trust/credentials' },
  { label: 'Marketplace',           route: '/marketplace' },
  { label: 'Analytics',             route: '/analytics' },
  { label: 'Career Growth',         route: '/career-growth',      note: 'PROFILE Advisor, Profile Health, Career Readiness Engine — §50.9' },
  { label: 'Learning Center',       route: '/learn',               note: 'PROFILE Academy — §50.9.7' },
  { label: 'Communities',           route: '/communities' },
  { label: 'Collaboration Hub',     route: '/collab' },
  { label: 'Recruiter Hub',         route: '/recruiter',           visibleTo: 'recruiter_and_organisation_accounts' },
  { label: 'Institution Hub',       route: '/institution',         visibleTo: 'verified_educational_institutions_only' }, // §10.6
  { label: 'Organisation Dashboard', route: '/org-dashboard',      visibleTo: 'organisation_accounts OR individuals_with_active_OrgAdminAssignment' },
  { label: 'Admin Centre',          route: '/admin',               visibleTo: 'authorised_roles_only' },
  { label: 'Developer Platform',    route: '/developers' },
  { label: 'API Centre',            route: '/api' },
  { label: 'Integrations',          route: '/integrations' },
  { divider: true },
  { label: 'Settings',              route: '/settings' },
  { label: 'Privacy & Security',    route: '/settings/security' },
  { label: 'Language Selector',     action: 'openLanguageSelector' },
  { label: 'Accessibility Options', route: '/settings?tab=accessibility' },  // §25 Accessibility tab
  { label: 'Help & Support',        route: '/help' },
  { label: 'About PROFILE',         route: '/explore#about' },
  { label: 'Feedback Centre',       route: '/feedback' },
  { label: 'Release Notes',         route: '/release-notes' },
  { divider: true },
  { label: 'Sign Out',              action: 'signOut' },
];
// RULE: this menu adapts to the signed-in account's permissions — an item a user cannot
// access for their role/verification status is not shown, not shown-and-disabled.
```

### 10.5 Account-Aware Navigation

Navigation is never one-size-fits-all — it changes structurally by account type, restated
here from §8.3/§8.4 specifically for its navigation consequences:

```typescript
// INDIVIDUAL
const INDIVIDUAL_BOTTOM_NAV = ['home', 'connections', 'create_activity', 'opportunity', 'cv' /* not portfolio-only */];

// ORGANISATION (Company/Startup/NGO/etc. — business_hub or community_hub per §8.4)
const ORGANISATION_BOTTOM_NAV = ['home', 'connections', 'create_activity', 'opportunity' /* posting + pipeline, not applying */, 'portfolio' /* never cv */];
const ORGANISATION_MODULES = ['portfolio', 'hiring_dashboard', 'verification_dashboard_if_authorised', 'marketplace', 'organisation_analytics'];

// INSTITUTION HUB — Universities, Polytechnics, and other credential-issuing institutions
// (institutional_hub per §8.4) — the ONLY organisation types with access to these tools.
const INSTITUTION_HUB_FEATURES = [
  'credential_validation', 'academic_verification', 'student_verification',
  'transcript_verification', 'graduation_verification', 'schema_builder' /* §16.2 */,
  'verification_workflows', 'institutional_reports',
];
// HARD RULE: no other organisation type — not Company, not NGO, not Government — receives
// Institution Hub access. Access is gated on registryLevel === 'verified' (§21 IOTR) AND
// organisationType mapping to institutional_hub (§8.4), both conditions required.

// GOVERNMENT & REGULATORY (regulatory_console per §8.4)
const GOVERNMENT_CONSOLE_FEATURES = [
  'national_credential_validation', 'regulatory_verification', 'institutional_accreditation',
  'audit_reports', 'trust_network_oversight',
];
```

### 10.6 Desktop Navigation — Three-Column Layout

```
LEFT SIDEBAR (persistent, ≥1440px full labels; collapsible icon-only 1024–1439px):
  Home · Connections · Opportunity Hub · Marketplace · Communities
  {/* Discover lives in the persistent global search (§10.2) on desktop too — not a
      separate sidebar item, consistent with the mobile rationale in §10.1. */}

CENTER COLUMN:
  Professional Activity Feed (§11) · Create Activity Composer (fixed, always visible) ·
  Opportunity Highlights

RIGHT SIDEBAR:
  Trust Score (§13) · Suggested Connections · Recommended Opportunities ·
  Trending Skills · Marketplace Highlights · Recent Messages · Upcoming Interviews
```

```
Top strip fallback below 1024px (tablet). All areas reachable from bottom tabs + Profile
Drawer + Hamburger remain directly accessible on desktop too — nothing is mobile-only.
```

### 10.7 Universal Page Consistency

Every page, on every breakpoint, shares:

```typescript
const UNIVERSAL_PAGE_STRUCTURE = [
  'breadcrumb_navigation', 'clear_page_title', 'secondary_actions',
  'responsive_spacing', 'persistent_navigation', 'keyboard_accessibility', 'touch_optimisation',
];
// Icons: Lucide SVG only (§3.6), consistent sizing and spacing — never mixed icon sets.
// Colour tokens: §3.1 exclusively — Royal Blue / Deep Blue / Trust Cyan / Gold / semantic
// colours. No alternate palette is introduced by this navigation standard.
// Accessibility: WCAG 2.2 AA — keyboard navigation, screen readers, high-contrast mode,
// prefers-reduced-motion (§3.6 already requires this), large touch targets, adjustable
// text scaling.
```

### 10.8 Navigation Quality Gate

```
✓ Every navigation item resolves correctly — Route Integrity Engine (§49.3) applies
✓ Every page includes a route back to Home
✓ No hidden or scroll-obscured Create Activity button
✓ Individual, Organisation, Institution Hub, and Government Console differ appropriately
✓ Institution Hub restricted to verified educational institutions only (§10.5)
✓ Government Console restricted to authorised government accounts only (§10.5)
✓ Mobile and desktop navigation render from the same shared config — never two menus
```
