## 11. HOME FEED & RECORD WORK SYSTEM

### 11.0 Daily Return Engine (extends §50.9.2 PROFILE Advisor)

PROFILE Advisor (§50.9.2) already computes recommendations from `PROFILE_ADVISOR_EVALUATES`.
This adds the missing delivery surface: a widget at the top of Home that renders the top
2–4 of those recommendations at login, with their estimated impact on the Profile Health
Dashboard scores (§50.9.3). This is a rendering surface only — no new computation logic,
no second recommendation engine.

```typescript
interface DailyReturnWidget {
  greeting: string;                    // "Good morning, {firstName}."
  suggestedActions: {
    label: string;                     // e.g. "Verify your highest qualification"
    estimatedImpact: Partial<Record<keyof ProfileHealthScores, number>>; // reuses §50.9.3 score keys
  }[];                                  // 2–4 items, ranked by PROFILE Advisor
}
```

Governed by the same Non-Negotiable Design Constraint 2 (no feature solely to increase
time-on-platform, top of document) — every action shown must map to a real, existing
evidence gap in the user's profile; this widget is never populated with filler content.

### 11.1 Activity Card Structure

```tsx
<ActivityCard>
  <Header>
    <UserAvatar />
    <FullName />
    <VerifiedBadge condition={user.isVerified} />
    <PathwayBadge type={user.pathway} />
    <ActivityTypeBadge type={activity.type} />
    <TrustScoreRing score={user.trustScore.total} tier={user.trustScore.tier} />
  </Header>

  <Body>
    <Title>{activity.title}</Title>
    <Description>{activity.description}</Description>
    <SkillTags skills={activity.skills} />
    <ToolTags tools={activity.tools} />
    <MediaThumbnail copyright={`#PRF-${year}-${activity.copyrightId}`} />
    <CollaboratorsList collaborators={activity.collaborators} />
    <ClientReviewSnippet review={activity.clientReview} verified={true} />
  </Body>

  <Actions>
    <ProfessionalReactions reactions={SIX_REACTIONS} />
    <CommentSection />
    <ShareButton />
    <SelfTagButton />   {/* [+ Tag Me] */}
  </Actions>
</ActivityCard>
```

### 11.2 Six Professional Reactions

```typescript
// NOT vanity — professional acknowledgement signals
// Do NOT feed Trust Score · Do NOT drive ranking · No negative reactions
const SIX_REACTIONS = [
  { emoji: '👍', label: 'Appreciate',      meaning: 'I acknowledge and value this work' },
  { emoji: '🫡', label: 'Respect',         meaning: 'This demonstrates serious expertise or dedication' },
  { emoji: '💡', label: 'Insightful',     meaning: 'I gained knowledge or perspective from this' },
  { emoji: '🌱', label: 'Support',         meaning: 'I believe in this person or initiative' },
  { emoji: '⚒️', label: 'Skilled',        meaning: 'This demonstrates clear, verifiable technical skill' },
  { emoji: '🗣️', label: 'Well Explained', meaning: 'This was communicated with exceptional clarity' },
];

const REACTION_RULES = {
  onePerUserPerPost: true,
  selectable: true,
  changeable: true,
  feedsTrustScore: false,         // NEVER
  drivesRanking: false,           // NEVER
  negativeReactions: false,       // NONE exist
  totalsPublic: true,
  individualChoicesPublic: false,
};
```

### 11.3 Self-Tag System

```typescript
interface SelfTagRequest {
  requesterId:    string;
  activityId:     string;
  role:           string;
  status:         'requested' | 'pending_approval' | 'approved' | 'rejected';
}
// Approved: tag appears on tagged user's CV/Portfolio with role attribution
// Notifications: sent to tagged user + organisation (if org mentioned)
```

### 11.4 Record Work — Post Composer

```typescript
const POST_COMPOSER_TYPES = [
  'Project', 'Work Experience', 'Skill Demonstration', 'Certification Earned',
  'Achievement', 'Publication', 'Trade Job', 'Mentorship', 'Collaboration',
  'Event Participation', 'Volunteer Work', 'Portfolio Item',
];

const COMPOSER_FIELDS = {
  required:  ['type', 'title', 'description', 'role', 'dateRange'],
  optional:  ['organisation', 'client', 'skills', 'tools', 'media', 'collaborators'],
  toggles:   {
    cvPortfolioInclude: { default: true, label: 'Include in CV / Portfolio' },
    visibility:         { options: ['public', 'connections_only', 'private'], default: 'public' },
  },
};

// Media upload spec ( — extends the `media` composer field above; was previously
// unspecified beyond the Copyright System below):
const COMPOSER_UPLOAD_SPEC = {
  acceptedTypes: [
    'image', 'video', 'audio', 'pdf', 'docx', 'pptx', 'xlsx',
    'research_paper', 'epub', 'mobi', 'zip',
  ],
  maxFileSize: '20GB',
  behavior: ['progress_indicator', 'thumbnail_generation', 'client_side_compression', 'resumable_uploads'],
};
// Feeds the existing Copyright System and offline `uploadQueue` (§29.2) unchanged — this
// is a spec addition on the existing media pipeline, not a second upload system.

// Organisation Mention System:
// When org name typed → check if registered on PROFILE
// If registered → org receives in-app + email notification:
// "[User Name] referenced [Organisation] in a professional activity. Review and verify."
// Org can: Approve · Reject · Request Evidence · Ignore

// Copyright System:
// Every media upload → Copyright ID: #PRF-YYYY-XXXX
// Content hash + ownership fingerprint + metadata timestamp stored
// Duplicate upload detection: automatic
// Revenue split: 90% creator / 10% platform
```

## 12. TRUST CENTER (FLAGSHIP SYSTEM)

### 12.1 Trust Center — Merged Modules

The Trust Center is the merger of: **Integrity Centre + Credential Hub + Verification Centre**

```
ZERO credential uploads, institutional identifiers, or verification documents
belong anywhere in registration or onboarding.
ALL of it lives exclusively in the Trust Center.
```

### 12.2 Trust Center Modules

```typescript
const TRUST_CENTER_MODULES = {
  1: {
    name: 'Identity Verification',
    accepts: ['National ID', 'Passport', 'Driver Licence', 'Government ID', 'Residence Permit', 'Tax ID', 'Professional ID'],
    process: 'document_validation + liveness_check (regional) + issuing_authority_routing',
  },
  2: {
    name: 'Academic Verification',
    identifiers: ['Matriculation Number', 'Student ID', 'Degree Reference Number', 'Alumni Record Number', 'Transcript Number'],
    route: 'institution → dashboard_alert + email → verification_queue',
    institutionActions: ['Approve', 'Reject', 'Request Evidence', 'Escalate', 'Revoke'],
  },
  3: {
    name: 'Employment Verification',
    identifiers: ['Employee ID', 'Staff Number', 'HR Reference', 'Payroll ID', 'Contract Number'],
    route: 'employer registered PROFILE account or official HR contact',
    employerActions: ['Approve', 'Reject', 'Request Evidence'],
  },
  4: {
    name: 'Professional Licence Verification',
    routes_to: ['Medical councils', 'Bar associations', 'Engineering boards', 'Accounting bodies', 'Government regulatory agencies', 'Trade licensing authorities'],
    identifiers: ['Licence Number', 'Registration Number', 'Practice Number'],
  },
  5: {
    name: 'Trade & Competence Verification',
    sources: ['Guild', 'Master craftsperson', 'Apprenticeship record', 'Workshop participation', 'Trade certification', 'Equipment records'],
    verified_through: ['Guild organisations', 'Community validation', 'Portfolio-based evidence'],
  },
  6: {
    name: 'Certification Verification',
    categories: ['Professional', 'Technical', 'Academic', 'Industry', 'Vendor', 'Government', 'NGO', 'International'],
    examples: ['AWS', 'Microsoft', 'Google', 'Cisco', 'Autodesk', 'PMI', 'Coursera', 'Universities', 'Trade Schools'],
  },
  7: {
    name: 'Universal Custom Verification',
    description: 'For any credential not in predefined categories',
    ifInstitutionNotRegistered: 'invitation sent to join PROFILE and respond',
  },
};
```

### 12.3 Universal Verification Identifier Framework (UVIF)

```typescript
// PROFILE never assumes a single identifier type
const UVIF_IDENTIFIERS = {
  academic:     ['Student ID', 'Matric Number', 'Candidate Number', 'Graduation Number', 'Transcript Number', 'Degree Reference'],
  employment:   ['Staff ID', 'Employee Number', 'Payroll Number', 'HR Reference', 'Contract Number'],
  certification:['Certificate Number', 'Candidate Number', 'Certification ID', 'Validation Code', 'Exam Number'],
  membership:   ['Membership Number', 'Registration Number', 'Licence Number', 'Practice Number'],
  government:   ['Registration Number', 'Tax Number', 'Permit Number', 'National ID'],
  business:     ['Company Registration', 'Business Licence', 'Tax Registration'],
  custom:       'Institution-defined identifier fields',
};
```

### 12.4 Verification Timeline (Live Visual Per Submission)

```
○ Submitted
  ↓
○ Received
  ↓
○ Institution Notified
  ↓
○ Under Review
  ↓
○ Approved
  ↓
✓ Verified → Active
             ↘ Rejected → Appeal Available
             ↘ Escalated → Human Review → Expired / Renewal Required
```

### 12.5 Credential Vault

```typescript
interface CredentialVault {
  storage:          'encrypted_permanent';
  features: [
    'full_verification_history_per_credential',
    'renewal_reminders_configurable',
    'ownership_records_with_cryptographic_hash',
    'verification_qr_code_scannable_publicly_verifiable_url',
    'issuing_authority_signature',
    'trust_weight_contribution_record',
    'audit_log_all_credential_events',
    'public_validation_link',
  ];
}
```

### 12.6 Trust Passport™

```typescript
interface TrustPassport {
  includes: [
    'Identity Verification',
    'Academic Verification',
    'Employment Verification',
    'Certification Verification',
    'Contribution Verification',
    'Organisation Verification',
    'Activity Integrity',
    'Trust History',
  ];
  accessPoints: [
    'PROFILE URL',
    'QR Code',
    'Verification Link',
    'Recruiter-Specific View',
    'Institution-Specific View',
  ];
  visibilityControls: {
    perShareLink: true;   // NOT global — user sets per link
    presets: ['full_visibility', 'recruiter_view', 'institution_view', 'custom_field_by_field'];
  };
}
```

## 13. TRUST SCORE ARCHITECTURE

### 13.1 Score Range & Tiers

```typescript
const TRUST_TIERS = [
  { range: [0, 199],    tier: 'emerging',     label: 'Emerging',     color: '#94A3B8' },
  { range: [200, 399],  tier: 'established',  label: 'Established',  color: '#0057D9' },  // Royal Blue, §3.1
  { range: [400, 599],  tier: 'trusted',      label: 'Trusted',      color: '#00E5FF' },  // Trust Cyan, §3.1/§3.11
  { range: [600, 799],  tier: 'expert',       label: 'Expert',       color: '#003A8C' },  // Deep Blue, §3.1
  { range: [800, 1000], tier: 'master',       label: 'Master',       color: '#F4B400' },  // Gold — the one tier where the restricted Achievement accent (§3.1, §3.9) is earned, not just displayed
];

const TRUST_CONFIDENCE = ['low', 'moderate', 'high', 'institutional_grade'];
```

### 13.2 Trust Signal Weights

```typescript
const TRUST_WEIGHTS = {
  identity_verification:          25,
  employment_verification:        25,
  academic_degree_verification:   20,
  professional_licence:           20,
  organisation_affiliation:       15,
  trade_competence:               15,
  certification:                  12,
  client_review_verified:         10,
  peer_endorsement_higher_tier:    8,
  course_completed:                8,
  activity_peer_validated:         5,
};
// HARD RULE: No follower count, like count, view count, share count,
// or any derivative may appear as a ranking input. Zero exceptions.
```

### 13.3 Transparency Rules

```typescript
// Every increment shown to user with source:
// "+25 Employment Verified by GlobalTech Inc · +20 Degree Verified by University of Lagos"
// Users always know EXACTLY why their score changed.

// Versioning requirement:
interface TrustScoreVersion {
  version:   string;
  effectiveDate: Date;
  weights:   typeof TRUST_WEIGHTS;
  // Historical scores reconstructable under formula active when calculated
  // Required for: dispute resolution + regulatory scrutiny
}

// Trust Decay:
// Unverified, expired, or disputed signals decay at configurable rates
// Ensures trust reflects current credibility, not historical claims
```

## 15. CV / PORTFOLIO AUTO-BUILD ENGINE

### 15.1 Core Principles

```
CVs and Portfolios are NEVER assembled manually from scratch.
They are auto-generated, continuously updated, evidence-driven,
and recruiter-ready at all times.

CRITICAL NON-NEGOTIABLE:
AI-generated CVs and Portfolios MUST remain fully editable at all times.
The AI is an assistant. The user is ALWAYS the owner and editor.

Users may: Edit · Add Sections · Remove Sections · Reorder Sections ·
           Change Layouts · Change Templates · Customize Content ·
           Create Multiple Versions · Switch Templates · Control Visibility
```

### 15.2 Pathway-Aware Output

```typescript
const CV_PATHWAYS = {
  professional: {
    name: 'Professional CV',
    sections: [
      'ai_generated_profile_summary_editable',
      'work_experience_timeline_auto_populated',
      'education_from_trust_center',
      'core_skills_with_proficiency_visualisation',
      'tools_and_software_proficiency_auto_detected',
      'certifications_with_verification_status_badges',
      'publications_and_research',
      'achievements_and_awards',
      'collaboration_history_with_attribution',
      'trust_score_panel',
    ],
  },
  competence: {
    name: 'Work Identity Portfolio',
    sections: [
      'who_i_am_narrative_ai_drafted_user_editable',
      'proof_of_work_timeline_chronological_evidence_linked',
      'trade_skills_auto_classified',

      'community_verifications',
      'apprenticeship_record',
      'micro_credentials_course_completions',
      'media_gallery_from_recorded_activities',
    ],
  },
  hybrid: {
    // 'hybrid' is a valid pathway value (§8.3: "Engineer &
    // Fabricator," "Doctor & Entrepreneur," "Architect & Builder," "Lecturer & Consultant")
    // but had no corresponding CV template. This merges the professional and competence
    // section sets rather than inventing a third parallel structure.
    name: 'Hybrid Professional & Competence CV',
    sections: [
      'ai_generated_profile_summary_editable',
      'work_experience_timeline_auto_populated',
      'proof_of_work_timeline_chronological_evidence_linked',
      'education_from_trust_center',

      'collaboration_history_with_attribution',
      'trust_score_panel',
    ],
  },
  organisation: {
    name: 'Institutional Portfolio',
    sections: [
      'verified_description_and_overview',
      'programs_and_initiatives_timeline',
      'staff_highlights_verified',
      'research_publications',
      'community_impact_records',
      'partnerships_and_mous',
      'recruitment_campaigns',
    ],
  },
};
```

### 15.3 CV Interface Features

```typescript
const CV_INTERFACE = {
  templates:    10,               // recruiter-grade, pathway-aware defaults, fully switchable
  sectionToggle: true,
  livePreview:  true,
  pdfDownload:  'premium',
  webUrl:       'myprofile.com/@username',  // consumer platform domain, §50.8
  qrCode:       { embeds: 'PROFILE_logo', downloadable: true },
  recruiterView: 'optimised_read_only_for_sharing',
  atsCompatible: true,
  aiSuggestions: 'non_mandatory',
  shareLink:    { accessControls: true },
  multipleVersions: 'premium',
  dragDropReorder:  true,
};
```

### 15.4 Portfolio Display — View Modes & Mandatory Visibility

```
HARD RULE: Portfolio is mandatory-visible to anyone who can view the profile at all. Unlike
CV (§15.3's cvVisibility — public, private, or organisations-only, a real user preference),
Portfolio has no equivalent toggle. Someone visiting a PROFILE page always sees the
Portfolio. What remains controllable is per-item: an individual Activity's own visibility
(§4.3's existing 'public' | 'connections' | 'private', and includeInPortfolio) still governs
whether that specific piece of work appears at all — a person curates what's in their
Portfolio by choosing what to record and include, not by hiding the Portfolio itself.

Portfolio is the complete archive — every Activity with includeInPortfolio = true (default
true, §4.3), not a curated highlights reel. The Profile page's existing <FeaturedProjects
pinned /> (§24.1) remains the curated, pin-a-few-favorites view shown inline on the profile;
Portfolio is the full, browsable collection behind it, reached via the CV/Portfolio nav tab
(§10.1).
```

```typescript
type PortfolioViewMode = 'thumbnail' | 'grid' | 'card_flip';

interface PortfolioDisplaySettings {
  viewMode: PortfolioViewMode;        // user-controlled, persisted per-viewer (§15.4.1) —
                                        // not forced on the profile owner's audience
  defaultViewMode: PortfolioViewMode;  // 'grid' — the safest default across all three: denser
                                        // than thumbnail, simpler to render than card_flip
}

interface PortfolioCard {
  activityId:   string;                // sources directly from Activity (§4.3) — no separate schema
  media:        MediaAsset | null;
  title:        string;
  description?: string;                // clamped to 6 lines in thumbnail/grid views (§15.4.2);
                                        // shown only if the underlying Activity actually has one —
                                        // no placeholder text, no empty description block
  activityType: ActivityType;
  verified:     boolean;
}
```

### 15.4.1 View Switcher

```
The switcher itself is a compact control — visually, six small squares/dots (a familiar
"view options" affordance, distinct from the Hamburger/Ecosystem Menu's icon at §10.4 so the
two are never confused) — opening exactly three options:

  Thumbnail  — image-forward, minimal text, dense wall-of-work browsing
  Grid       — the default; balanced image + title + description, even columns
  Card Flip  — media on the front face, title + full description revealed on flip/hover/tap

Persisted per-viewer, not per-profile-owner: a recruiter viewing ten different Portfolios in
one session keeps their own chosen view mode across all of them, rather than each profile
owner's last-used mode overriding the visitor's preference.
```

### 15.4.2 Card Content Rules

```
Every card, in every view mode, shows Title first, always. Description follows only if the
source Activity actually has one (§4.3's description field) — clamped to 6 lines with a
"show more" affordance, never silently truncated with no way to read the rest. Media
(§4.3's MediaAsset[]) fills the card's visual anchor; an Activity with no media yet still
renders as a text-forward card, never a broken image placeholder.
```

## 16. INSTITUTIONAL TRUST HUB & VXI

### 16.1 Core Vision

```
PROFILE IS NOT THE VERIFIER. PROFILE IS THE ORCHESTRATOR.
PROFILE is: trust infrastructure · routing layer · evidence layer ·
            verification orchestration engine · compliance layer

Users ↔ Institutions ↔ Organisations ↔ Recruiters ↔ Governments ↔ Opportunity Providers
```

### 16.2 Institutional Schema Builder

```typescript
// Institutions define their OWN verification requirements — no fixed PROFILE template
interface VerificationSchema {
  id:           string;
  institutionId: string;
  name:         string;
  version:      string;             // version-controlled
  fields:       SchemaField[];
  matchingRule: SchemaMatchingRule;  // NEW — governs how submitted fields are matched at scale (§16.4)
  verificationType: VerificationType; // NEW — which precise claim this schema confirms (§4.4's split types).
                                       // A schema built for 'academic_affiliation' cannot silently upgrade
                                       // itself into confirming 'academic' (degree) — that requires either a
                                       // separate schema or an explicit, additional field group for it.
  status:       'draft' | 'published' | 'retired';
  createdAt:    Date;
}

interface SchemaField {
  name:       string;
  type:       'text' | 'number' | 'date' | 'select' | 'document';
  required:   boolean;
  validation: ValidationRule[];
  label:      string;               // localised
}

interface SchemaMatchingRule {
  // Order-independent, AND-of-OR-groups matching: every group below must have at least
  // one field that matches; which specific field matches within a group doesn't matter,
  // and the institution decides the groups — this is what lets "matric number OR
  // certificate number OR institutional email, AND legal name" be expressed directly,
  // rather than forcing one fixed field order onto every institution's actual records.
  requiredFieldGroups: string[][];   // e.g. [['fullLegalName'], ['studentNumber','institutionalEmail']]
  minimumConfidenceForAutoVerify: number; // reuses §16.4's existing 0–100 confidence scale directly —
                                           // no second scoring system
}

// Examples — two schemas, two different precise claims, same institution:
const UNIVERSITY_AFFILIATION_SCHEMA: VerificationSchema = {
  verificationType: 'academic_affiliation',   // confirms attendance ONLY — see Hard Rule at §4.4
  fields: [
    { name: 'fullLegalName',    required: true },
    { name: 'studentNumber',    required: false },
    { name: 'institutionalEmail', required: false },
    { name: 'graduationYear',   required: false },
  ],
  matchingRule: {
    requiredFieldGroups: [['fullLegalName'], ['studentNumber', 'institutionalEmail', 'graduationYear']],
    minimumConfidenceForAutoVerify: 90,
  },
  // This is the schema that lets a university confirm "these 50,000 people attended us"
  // safely at scale (§16.4 auto-verifies anything at or above the confidence threshold;
  // only genuine mismatches or ambiguous records reach a human) — without that confirmation
  // ever being displayable as a degree, because this schema's verificationType forbids it.
};

const UNIVERSITY_DEGREE_SCHEMA: VerificationSchema = {
  verificationType: 'academic',               // confirms a specific awarded degree
  fields: [
    { name: 'fullLegalName',    required: true },
    { name: 'studentNumber',    required: true },
    { name: 'degree',           required: true },
    { name: 'faculty',          required: true },
    { name: 'department',       required: false },
    { name: 'graduationYear',   required: true },
  ],
  matchingRule: {
    requiredFieldGroups: [['fullLegalName'], ['studentNumber'], ['degree'], ['graduationYear']],
    minimumConfidenceForAutoVerify: 95,        // higher bar — a degree claim carries more weight (§13.2)
                                                // than an affiliation claim, so it earns a stricter threshold
  },
};
```

```typescript
const MATCHING_STAGES = [
  { stage: 1, name: 'Schema Matching',      action: 'Determine institution requirements' },
  { stage: 2, name: 'Identifier Matching',  action: 'Compare Name · Verification ID · Credential Type · Date · Reference Numbers' },
  { stage: 3, name: 'Confidence Scoring',   action: 'Exact Match / Partial Match / Ambiguous Match → Confidence Score 0–100' },
  { stage: 4, name: 'Verification Routing', action: 'Auto-verify if ≥ threshold OR route to Manual Review' },
];

// AI-Assisted Matching:
// Handles: spelling errors · nickname variations · transliteration differences ·
//          regional naming · partial identity matches
// Example: Mohammed / Muhammad / Mohamad → intelligently resolved
```

## 17. DISCOVER & SMART SEARCH

### 17.1 Hard Rule

```
NO follower count · like count · view count · share count
or any derivative may appear as a ranking input in Discovery.
ZERO exceptions. ZERO configuration flags.
```

### 17.2 Discovery Ranking Signals (Priority Order)

```typescript
const RANKING_SIGNALS = [
  { rank: 1,  signal: 'Trust Score' },
  { rank: 2,  signal: 'Verification Status' },
  // Below 1 & 2: weighted composite score (not 10 independent inputs)
  { rank: 3,  signal: 'Credential Quality' },
  { rank: 4,  signal: 'Project Quality' },
  { rank: 5,  signal: 'Portfolio Strength' },
  { rank: 6,  signal: 'Verified Experience' },
  { rank: 7,  signal: 'Skills' },
  { rank: 8,  signal: 'Tools Used' },
  { rank: 9,  signal: 'Institutional Recognition' },
  { rank: 10, signal: 'Verified Contributions' },
  { rank: 11, signal: 'Professional Recommendations' },
  { rank: 12, signal: 'Opportunity Performance' },
];
// Both pathways (Academic + Competency) ALWAYS ranked equally — non-negotiable
```

## 18. OPPORTUNITY HUB

### 18.1 Core Principle

```
PROFILE IS the application. Users NEVER upload CVs to apply.
Their verified PROFILE is submitted. One-tap application.
```

### 18.2 Job Listings

```typescript
const JOB_LISTING_FIELDS = {
  required: ['title', 'organisation', 'location_remote', 'type', 'salary_rate', 'requiredSkills', 'minimumVerificationLevel', 'minimumTrustTier', 'deadline', 'description'],
  types: ['remote', 'hybrid', 'on_site'],
};

const APPLY_WITH_PROFILE = {
  method: 'one_tap_application',
  submits: 'verified CV + trust score + relevant activities',
  tracking: 'personal Kanban pipeline',
};

// Ranking rationale note (): as a profile's identity strength (Trust Score §13,
// verification depth §12) rises, it surfaces more often across Discovery (§17) and
// Opportunity matching — inbound visibility compounds with verified evidence rather
// than with activity volume or attention metrics, consistent with §17.1's Hard Rule.
// No new ranking mechanism — this restates §17.2's existing RANKING_SIGNALS priority
// order (Trust Score first, Verification Status second) as it plays out over time.
```

### 18.3 Recruiter Mode (Full Hiring OS)

```typescript
const KANBAN_PIPELINE = ['Applied', 'Shortlisted', 'Interview Scheduled', 'Offer Made', 'Hired'];

const RECRUITER_TOOLS = [
  'candidate_profile_review_with_trust_score_breakdown',
  'skill_gap_analysis_against_job_requirements',
  'side_by_side_candidate_comparison',
  'interview_scheduling_calendar_integration',
  'recruiter_to_candidate_encrypted_messaging',
  'ai_shortlisting_recommendations',
  'team_collaboration_on_candidate_notes',
  'contract_generation_and_e_signature',
  'hiring_analytics_and_pipeline_metrics',
];

// Trust Search (Recruiter-specific):
const TRUST_FILTERS = [
  'Verified Identity',
  'Verified Education',
  'Verified Employment',
  'Verified Licenses',
  'Verified Certifications',
  'Verified Skills',
  'Verified References',
  'Verified Projects',
];

// note — careerStage was surfaced in Graduate Launchpad / Student Opportunity
// Hub (§18.5) but never made filterable from the recruiter side, so a recruiter running an
// internship or graduate programme had no way to search specifically for that pool. Extends
// TRUST_FILTERS rather than forking a second candidate-search system.
//
// note — the  fix only mapped 15 of the 20 CAREER_STAGE_OPTIONS (§8.3) into
// a cluster, leaving 'apprentice', 'career_transition', 'mentor', and 'retired_professional'
// unfilterable by any recruiter — an onboarding-captured value with no corresponding search
// path. Closed by adding two clusters, aligned one-for-one with §50.9.5's Role-Specific
// Guidance table rows so the filter taxonomy and the guidance taxonomy never drift apart
// again. All 20 CareerStage values now resolve to exactly one cluster below.
const CAREER_STAGE_FILTER = {
  filterKey: 'careerStageCluster',
  options: [
    { label: 'Students & Learners', clusters: ['learner', 'student', 'undergraduate', 'technical_vocational_learner'], linksTo: '/opportunity/students' },
    { label: 'Graduates & Entry-Level', clusters: ['emerging_professional', 'fresh_graduate', 'job_seeker', 'intern', 'national_service_participant'], linksTo: '/opportunity/graduates' },
    { label: 'Apprentices & Skilled Trades', clusters: ['apprentice', 'skilled_tradesperson'] }, // mirrors §50.9.5's "Skilled Tradesperson" row (evidence-first, same as §8.3 Competence)
    { label: 'Experienced Professionals', clusters: ['professional', 'freelancer', 'entrepreneur', 'researcher', 'consultant', 'executive'] },
    { label: 'Career Changers', clusters: ['career_transition'] }, // new cluster, previously unfilterable
    { label: 'Mentors & Advisors', clusters: ['mentor', 'retired_professional'] }, // mirrors §50.9.5's "Mentor / Retired Professional" row; see §50.9.8 Mentorship
  ],
};
// A listing filed under §18.4's ACADEMIC_OPPORTUNITIES (e.g. 'Internships') or flagged as
// a graduate programme auto-applies the matching cluster filter and surfaces in Graduate
// Launchpad / Student Opportunity Hub (§18.5) for that candidate pool — closing the loop
// in both directions. (Not the same as JOB_LISTING_FIELDS.types above, which governs work
// arrangement — remote/hybrid/on-site — not candidate experience level.)
// Coverage check: 4 + 5 + 2 + 6 + 1 + 2 = 20 — matches CAREER_STAGE_OPTIONS (§8.3) exactly,
// with zero onboarding-capturable values left unfilterable.
```

### 18.4 Academic Opportunity Engine

```typescript
const ACADEMIC_OPPORTUNITIES = [
  'Scholarships', 'Internships', 'Research Grants', 'Assistantships',
  'Academic Placements', 'Fellowships', 'Exchange Programs',
  'Research Collaborations', 'Training Opportunities', 'Competitions',

```

### 18.5 Student Opportunity Hub & Graduate Launchpad — Named Routes

Open Item 10 (§50.9.10a) asked whether these two named surfaces should be promoted to
dedicated routes. Resolved: **yes, as sub-routes of Opportunity Hub**, not as new bottom-nav
tabs or Hamburger Menu entries — this keeps the Rule 4 navigation exception scoped to §10.1
exactly as originally authorized, while still making the two surfaces directly linkable,
bookmarkable, and shareable (e.g. by a university career office or a recruiter running a
graduate programme).

```typescript
const OPPORTUNITY_HUB_SUB_ROUTES = {
  studentOpportunityHub: {
    route: '/opportunity/students',
    title: 'Student Opportunity Hub',
    filtersTo: ACADEMIC_OPPORTUNITIES,                 // §18.4, unchanged taxonomy
    careerStageCluster: ['learner', 'student', 'undergraduate', 'technical_vocational_learner'],
    entryPoints: [
      'Opportunity Hub — pinned filter chip, always visible to matching careerStage users',
      'Home (§11.0 Daily Return Engine) — surfaced automatically for matching careerStage users',
      'Institution Hub (§16) — universities can deep-link students directly here',
    ],
  },
  graduateLaunchpad: {
    route: '/opportunity/graduates',
    title: 'Graduate Launchpad',
    filtersTo: ACADEMIC_OPPORTUNITIES.concat(['Entry-Level Roles', 'Graduate Programmes']),
    careerStageCluster: ['emerging_professional', 'fresh_graduate', 'job_seeker', 'intern', 'national_service_participant'],
    composedWith: 'SkillGapAnalysis + OpportunityPrediction (§50.9.4), rendered inline on the same page',
    entryPoints: [
      'Opportunity Hub — pinned filter chip, always visible to matching careerStage users',
      'Home (§11.0 Daily Return Engine) — surfaced automatically for matching careerStage users',
      'Career Growth (§50.9) — cross-linked from the Career Readiness Engine widget',
    ],
  },
};
// Both are views over §18's existing opportunity data — no new opportunity schema, no new
// application/matching logic. A user not in the matching careerStage cluster can still reach
// either route directly; the cluster only controls automatic surfacing, not access.
```

## 23. ANALYTICS

### 23.1 Individual Analytics (Premium)

```typescript
// Note: "Premium" here governs this page's reporting and trend data only — profile view
// history, network maps, recruiter-interest trends over time. PROFILE Advisor's live
// recommendations and Career Readiness Engine output are a different, permanently free
// capability (§50.9.2) that happens to also surface a summary here; their primary home is
// the free Daily Return Engine on Home (§11.0), not this page.
```

```tsx
    <RecentActivities />
    <FeaturedProjects pinned />
    <Skills proficiencyLevels verificationStatus />

</ProfilePage>
```

**`<OrganizationsAndRoles />` (extends §4.2 `OrgStaffRecord`, no new schema):**
one entry per `OrgStaffRecord` held by this user with `status: 'active'`, showing
Organisation · Role/Position · Department · Employment Period · Verification Status. This is
a direct rendering of the affiliation records defined in §4.2 — not a new data model.

```typescript
    fields: ['name', 'headline', 'bio', 'location', 'phone', 'email', 'photo', 'cover_photo', 'public_url'],
  },
  PrivacyAndVisibility: {
    controls: [
      'profile_visibility', 'credential_visibility', 'trust_passport_visibility', 'verification_visibility',
      'cv_visibility' /* public | private | organisations_only — §15.4 */, 'activity_visibility',
      'message_permissions', 'recruiter_access_toggle',
      'download_permissions', 'search_engine_indexing', 'data_sharing_preferences', 'blocked_users', 'muted_users',
    ],
    portfolioIsNotHereOnPurpose: 'Portfolio has no visibility toggle — §15.4\'s Hard Rule makes it
      mandatory-visible to anyone who can view the profile at all. What IS controllable here is
      activity_visibility per item (§4.3\'s existing Activity.visibility), which lets someone
      exclude a specific piece of work from the Portfolio without hiding the Portfolio itself.',
    identityAndPresentation: 'context-specific presentation modes live at §25.1 (PrivacyMode);
      whether your legal name is shown publicly at all, and how employment gaps are disclosed,

  },
  Notifications: {
    perCategoryToggles: [
      'tags', 'verifications', 'reactions', 'connections', 'job_matches', 'reviews', 'system', 'marketing',
      'messages', 'trust_updates', 'recruiters', 'job_applications', 'institution_requests', 'marketplace',
      'communities', 'security_alerts',

  },
  CVAndExport: {
    controls: ['section_visibility', 'template_preference', 'auto_include_toggle', 'pathway_switch', 'pdf_preferences', 'portfolio_default_view_mode'],
    note: 'portfolio_default_view_mode (§15.4) is the fallback shown to a visitor who has
      never set their own view-mode preference. Per §15.4.1, once any visitor has explicitly
      chosen thumbnail/grid/card_flip for their own session, their choice always wins on
      every Portfolio they view afterward — the owner\'s default never overrides an
      established visitor preference, it only fills the gap before one exists.',
  },
  CareerJourney: {   // NEW — closes a real gap: journeyStage was set at onboarding (§8.8.1)
                      // but had no editable home afterward
    controls: ['career_stage', 'journey_stage', 'update_journey_progress'],
    note: 'journeyStage (§4.1) advances year to year for students, or stage to stage for
      independent professionals — this is where that update actually happens after onboarding',
  },
  CommerceAndSelling: {   // NEW — the §38.6a toggles needed an actual settings home
    controls: ['store_activated_toggle', 'selling_activated_toggle', 'accepted_commerce_terms_version', 'payout_details'],
    note: 'extends §38.6a exactly — the two-toggle Commerce Activation pattern (My Store,
      then Accept Payments) lives here, not as a hidden Marketplace-page-only control',
  },
  AIAssistant: {   // NEW — §48.10 promised "user can fully disable the agent at any time";
                    // this is where that promise becomes an actual control
    controls: ['enable_disable_profile_advisor', 'daily_return_engine_frequency', 'ai_gateway_data_usage_disclosure'],
  },
  Mentorship: {   // NEW — §21.2/§50.9.8 Mentorship had no discoverability surface at all
    controls: ['open_to_mentoring_toggle', 'mentorship_availability', 'mentee_capacity'],
  },
  Security: {
    controls: ['password_change', 'active_sessions', 'trusted_devices', 'login_history', 'mfa_setup',
      'suspicious_login_alerts', 'lock_verified_activities', 'recovery_codes', 'connected_accounts',
      'connected_applications', 'account_deletion_workflow'],
    note: 'Lock verified activities: re-editing removes verification badge. connected_applications
      links to §37.26.5; account_deletion_workflow links to §37.26.6.',
  },
  LanguageAndRegion: {
    controls: ['preferred_languages', 'primary_language', 'regional_date_number_formatting'],
    phase1Languages: ['English (default)', 'French', 'Arabic', 'Mandarin Chinese', 'German', 'Spanish', 'Portuguese'],
    architecture: 'must_support_future_locales_without_code_rewrite',   // per §30
  },
  Accessibility: {   // NEW — closes a real stub: §10.4's Hamburger Menu already links to
                      // 'openAccessibilityPanel' with no panel ever specified until now
    controls: ['reduced_motion_override', 'high_contrast_mode', 'text_size_scaling', 'screen_reader_optimizations'],
  },

    controls: ['download_my_data', 'delete_my_account', 'withdraw_consent', 'request_correction', 'appeal_a_decision'],
    note: 'directly implements §27.4 COMPLIANCE.userRights (access, export, deletion,
      correction, consent_withdrawal, decision_appeal) as real, clickable controls',
  },
  PremiumPlans: {
    contains: ['free_vs_premium_comparison', 'upgrade_flow', 'billing_management', 'feature_unlock_status'],
    note: 'billing_management renders the Orders & Billing page defined at §38.6d — order
      history, invoices, saved payment methods, and the Subscription record (§38.6d), not
      an unspecified control',
  },
};
```

### 25.1 Advanced Privacy & Identity Controls

`PrivacyAndVisibility` above already covers baseline visibility toggles. This subsection
adds named, switchable **identity modes** — a user is not limited to one flat public/private
state, but can present a different professional face depending on context, all from the
same verified evidence base underneath.

**Relationship to §9.7 Private Professional Mode:** these are two different dimensions of
the same underlying privacy system, not two competing ones. `PrivacyMode` below controls
*which view* is shown to *which audience* (a recruiter sees `recruiter_mode`; a stranger
browsing sees `standard`). §9.7 controls a more fundamental question underneath all of
those views — *whether your legal name is shown at all*, and *whether an employment period
is disclosed publicly regardless of which PrivacyMode is active*. A person in
`anonymous_browsing` mode who also has `legalNamePublicVisibility: 'private'` (§9.7) gets
both protections layered together, not a choice between them.

```typescript
type PrivacyMode =
  | 'standard'            // default — full profile per baseline visibility settings above
  | 'portfolio_only'      // evidence and work shown; identity/contact fields suppressed
  | 'recruiter_mode'      // optimised, read-only view surfaced specifically to recruiters
  | 'founder_mode'        // emphasises organisation affiliation and ventures over employment history
  | 'anonymous_browsing'; // user can browse Discover (§17) and view others without appearing
                          // in "who viewed this profile" signals themselves

interface GranularVisibilityControls {
  hideEmployer: boolean;
  hideSalaryExpectations: boolean;
  hideSpecificProjects: string[];        // activity/portfolio item IDs, not a blanket toggle
  visibleTo: 'public' | 'collaborators_only' | 'recruiters_only' | 'organisations_only';
  cvVisibility: 'public' | 'private' | 'organisations_only';   // §15.4 — the CV's visibility is
                                          // a real user preference with exactly these three states.
                                          // This is deliberately different from Portfolio, which is
                                          // never private — see §15.4's Hard Rule.
}
```

A `PrivacyMode` switch changes *presentation*, never the underlying verified evidence —
switching to `portfolio_only` does not delete or hide verification records from the
account owner, only from the audience the mode is scoped to. Combined with §47.9's
sensitive-identifier rules, this gives users context-appropriate identities (e.g. a
`founder_mode` view shared with investors vs. a `recruiter_mode` view shared with a
prospective employer) without maintaining separate accounts.

### 25.2 Organisation Settings (parallel structure, Organisation accounts only — not
§26 Admin Centre, which is platform-staff-only and a different audience entirely)

```typescript
// §26's ADMIN_MODULES are for PROFILE's own platform staff (Super Admins, Verification
// Officers). An ordinary business managing its own organisation account needs a self-service
// settings surface too — this is that home, reached from the Organisation Dashboard
// (§10.4's 'Organisation Dashboard' menu entry), mirroring §25's structure for individuals.
const ORGANISATION_SETTINGS_TABS = {
  Profile: {
    fields: ['legalName', 'displayName', 'logo', 'coverBanner', 'website', 'officialContact'],  // §4.2
  },
  TeamAndRoles: {
    controls: ['staff_directory', 'promote_to_admin_role', 'pending_affiliation_requests'],  // §42
  },
  Commerce: {
    controls: ['direct_connect_opt_in', 'direct_pitch_preferences', 'charge_for_verification_toggle', 'verification_fee_schedule'],
    note: 'directly surfaces §39.13 openToDirectPitches/directPitchPreferences and §38.9
      InstitutionCommerceSettings.chargeForVerification — both existed as schema fields
      with no settings-page home until now',
  },
  VerificationSchemas: {
    controls: ['schema_builder', 'published_schemas', 'matching_rule_configuration'],  // §16.2
  },
  Billing: {
    contains: ['plan_management', 'invoice_history', 'payout_details'],  // §32.2
    note: 'invoice_history renders Invoice[] and Order[] (§38.6d), scoped to this
      organisation as sellerId/ownerId; plan_management operates on the same
      Subscription record (§38.6d) individuals use, just scoped to the OPID',

};
```

## 27. SECURITY ARCHITECTURE

### 27.1 Authentication Security

```typescript
// Restates and extends §7.2's AUTH_SECURITY with the session/MFA fields that section
// doesn't cover — renamed to avoid redeclaring the same identifier with a different shape.
const AUTH_SECURITY_OVERVIEW = {
  sessionManagement:  'rotation_with_expiration_and_refresh',
  mfa:               'TOTP_plus_SMS_ready',
  suspiciousLogin:   'step_up_challenge',                      // detailed at §7.2
  bruteForce:        'lockout_after_configurable_attempts',    // detailed at §7.2
  csrf:              'tokens_on_all_auth_requests',             // detailed at §7.2
  rateLimit:         'all_auth_endpoints',                      // detailed at §7.2
  deviceFingerprint: true,
};
```

### 27.2 Data Protection

```typescript
const DATA_PROTECTION = {
  encryptionAtRest:    'AES-256',
  encryptionInTransit: 'TLS 1.3',
  accessControls:      'role_based',
  auditLogs:           'all_consequential_actions',
  uploadSanitisation:  true,
  virusScanning:       true,
  xssProtection:       true,
  sqlInjectionPrevention: true,
  csrfProtection:      'throughout',
};
// AES-256 and TLS 1.3 are the same encryption standards approved for handling classified
// government information up to TOP SECRET — "military-grade" is not marketing language
// here, it is the literal specification already in place. §9.7's Private Professional Mode
// builds its privacy guarantee on top of this encryption layer, not instead of it.
```

## 28. TRUST GOVERNANCE & COMMUNITY STANDARDS

### 28.1 Governance Philosophy

> *"Professional credibility grows through verified contribution and responsible participation."*
> Governance is: **Fair · Transparent · Institutional · Human**
> Never: authoritarian · arbitrary · algorithmically abusive · opaque

### 28.2 Prohibited Identity Conduct

```
Users & Organisations MUST NOT:
✗ Impersonate individuals or organisations
✗ Create false verification claims or fake credentials
✗ Manipulate trust scores or verification systems
✗ Falsify work history, certifications, or institutional affiliations
✗ Create verification rings or review exchange networks
✗ Use bot-generated engagement or synthetic identities
✗ Upload others' work to the copyright system as their own
```

### 28.3 Prohibited Conduct (All Account Types)

```
harassment · threats · hate speech · doxxing · extortion · blackmail ·
sexual exploitation · scam activity · predatory recruitment · stalking ·
coercion · malicious misinformation · abusive negotiations ·
fake reviews · review manipulation · paid trust manipulation
```

### 28.4 Enforcement Escalation

```typescript
// All actions: documented · explainable · appealable within 14 days
const ENFORCEMENT_LEVELS = [
  { level: 1, action: 'Warning with documented explanation' },
  { level: 2, action: 'Temporary feature restriction' },
  { level: 3, action: 'Visibility reduction' },
  { level: 4, action: 'Verification revocation' },
  { level: 5, action: 'Account suspension' },
  { level: 6, action: 'Permanent removal' },
  { level: 7, action: 'Legal escalation (where required by law)' },
];
```

## 31. STATIC PAGES & LEGAL DOCUMENTS

All documents: **fully responsive · searchable · SEO optimised · Dark + Light mode · Markdown/CMS-ready · Version history · Effective date tracking · Multi-language ready**

Accessible from: **Footer · Settings · Trust Center · Verification Center · Registration Flow · Organisation Dashboard · Institutional Trust Hub · Public PROFILE URLs**

|---|---|---|
| About PROFILE | `/about` | Public |
| How PROFILE Works | `/how-it-works` | Public |
| Trust Center | `/trust` | Public + Auth |
| Privacy Policy | `/privacy` | Public |
| Terms of Service | `/terms` | Public |
| Identity & Authenticity Agreement | `/identity-agreement` | Public |
| Verification Routing Consent | `/verification-consent` | Public |
| Professional Conduct Standards | `/conduct` | Public |
| Data Processing Agreement (DPA) | `/dpa` | Public |
| Cookie Policy | `/cookies` | Public |
| Help Centre | `/help` | Public + Auth |

### 31.1 How PROFILE Works — Sections

```
1. Create Account
2. Build CV / Portfolio
3. Connect Institutions
4. Upload Credentials to Trust Center
5. Request Verification
6. Receive Verified Badges
7. Increase Trust Score
8. Become Discoverable
9. Access Opportunities
10. Earn Through Your Verified Identity
```

## 33. ANIMATION & INTERACTION SPECIFICATION

### 33.1 Animation Principles

```typescript
const ANIMATION_PRINCIPLES = {
  feel:       'smooth, purposeful, premium — never aggressive or distracting',
  library:    'Framer Motion for component transitions and reveals',
  scroll:     'Intersection Observer for scroll-triggered animations',
  parallax:   'depth on hero background elements',
  micro:      'microinteractions on every interactive element',
  loading:    'skeleton loading states (NEVER raw spinners)',
  motion:     'all animations respect prefers-reduced-motion',
  gpu:        'transform and opacity only for GPU acceleration',
};
```

### 33.2 Key Animation Templates

```typescript
// Framer Motion variants — use consistently
const FADE_UP = {
  hidden:  { opacity: 0, y: 20 },
  visible: { opacity: 1, y: 0, transition: { duration: 0.5, ease: 'easeOut' } },
};

const STAGGER_CHILDREN = {
  visible: { transition: { staggerChildren: 0.1 } },
};

const CARD_HOVER = {
  scale:     1.01,
  boxShadow: '0 8px 30px rgba(0,0,0,0.15)',
  transition: { duration: 0.2, ease: 'easeOut' },
};

const TRUST_SCORE_RING = {
  // SVG circle with strokeDashoffset animation.
  // First-ever render (no prior known score, e.g. a brand-new account): animates from 0.
  // Every subsequent render: animates from the last score PROFILE actually showed this
  // person (stored client-side / in session, not a new schema field) to the current score
  // — so a returning visit visibly shows growth since last time, per §3.6b, rather than
  // replaying the same 0-to-current animation every single login.
  animatesFrom: 'lastDisplayedScore ?? 0',
  animatesTo: 'score / 1000 * circumference',
  duration: 1.5,
  ease: 'easeOut',
  delay: 0.3,
};

const HERO_WORD_REVEAL = {
  // Word-by-word stagger on headline
  hidden:  { opacity: 0, y: 30 },
  visible: { opacity: 1, y: 0 },
  stagger: 0.08,
};

const MODAL_TRANSITION = {
  initial:  { opacity: 0, scale: 0.95 },
  animate:  { opacity: 1, scale: 1, transition: { duration: 0.2 } },
  exit:     { opacity: 0, scale: 0.95, transition: { duration: 0.15 } },
};

const SKELETON_SHIMMER = {
  // Linear gradient shimmer on skeleton placeholders
  // background: linear-gradient(90deg, #1E293B 25%, #243447 50%, #1E293B 75%)
  backgroundSize: '400% 100%',
  animation: 'shimmer 1.5s ease infinite',
};

const TRUST_CHAIN_NODE = {
  // Sequential illumination on scroll
  hidden:  { opacity: 0.2, scale: 0.9 },
  visible: { opacity: 1,   scale: 1, transition: { duration: 0.4, ease: 'easeOut' } },
};

const VERIFICATION_PULSE = {
  // Repeating pulse on verified badges
  scale:    [1, 1.05, 1],
  opacity:  [1, 0.8, 1],
  duration: 2,
  repeat:   Infinity,
  ease:     'easeInOut',
};
```

### 33.3 Button State Styles

```typescript
const BUTTON_STATES = {
  default:  { background: 'var(--color-royal)',   color: '#FFF', cursor: 'pointer' },
  hover:    { background: 'var(--color-sapphire)', transform: 'translateY(-1px)', boxShadow: '0 4px 12px rgba(37,99,235,0.4)' },
  focus:    { outline: '2px solid var(--color-cyan)', outlineOffset: '2px' },
  pressed:  { transform: 'translateY(0px)', boxShadow: 'none' },
  loading:  { cursor: 'wait',     content: '<Spinner /> Loading...' },
  success:  { background: 'var(--color-success)', content: '✓ Done' },
  error:    { background: 'var(--color-danger)',  content: '✗ Try Again' },
  disabled: { opacity: 0.4,       cursor: 'not-allowed', pointerEvents: 'none' },
};
```

---

## 34. RESILIENCE & FAILSAFE ARCHITECTURE

### 34.1 Four-Layer Architecture

```
PROFILE MUST NEVER FAIL.

Layer 1 — Primary React Application
  Full React + ReactDOM + compiled application state.

Layer 2 — Production Build (Vite / Next.js)
  Pre-compiled, locally bundled.
  ZERO browser-side Babel CDN dependency.
  All JS assets self-contained.
  No external CDN runtime dependencies in production.

Layer 3 — Offline Recovery Renderer
  If primary React fails, auto-serve functional native fallback covering:
  Auth · Dashboard · CV/Portfolio · Discovery · Messaging ·
  Trust Center · Marketplace · Jobs · Analytics · Settings
  Users must NEVER see blank screens, infinite loaders, or white pages.

Layer 4 — Automatic Error Recovery
  Monitor: React failures · script failures · hydration errors · dependency failures
  Trigger automatic silent restoration.
  Users must NOT experience the recovery process.
```

### 34.2 Route Guards

```typescript
// Every protected route must implement:
const ROUTE_GUARD = {
  authCheck:           'before_render',
  onboardingCheck:     'if_authenticated_but_incomplete',
  redirectOnFail:      '/auth',
  statePreservation:   'return_url_in_query_param',
  noLoops:             true,
  noHeroRedirects:     true,
  loadingState:        'show_during_auth_check',
  sectorScoping:       'org_hub_modules_scoped_to_orgType_(§4.2)_—_see_34.2a',
};
```

### 34.2a Sector-Scoped Institution Hub Modules (extends §16 VXI, §4.2 OrgType — a
visibility rule on existing modules, not a second Institution Hub per sector)

```
An organisation only sees the Institution Hub tools relevant to its own orgType (§4.2) —
a university does not see government licensing-board tools it has no use for, and a
government agency does not see university transcript-verification tools built for a
different sector. This is a visibility rule on the one existing hub, not a reason to build
a separate hub per sector, which would fragment §16's verification engine into duplicate
copies over time.
```

```typescript
const SECTOR_MODULE_VISIBILITY: Record<string, string[]> = {
  university:            ['academic_verification_(§12.2)', 'bulk_student_ingestion_(§16.3)', 'Institution_Excellence_Dashboard_(§50.9.12)'],
  school:                ['academic_verification_(§12.2)', 'bulk_student_ingestion_(§16.3)'],
  government_agency:     ['country_adapter_tools_(§16.6)', 'IOTR_(§50.4)', 'regulatory_licensing_workflows'],
  regulatory_body:       ['professional_licence_verification_(§12.2)', 'IOTR_(§50.4)'],
  hospital:              ['employment_verification_(§12.2)', 'professional_licence_verification_(§12.2)'],
  company:               ['employment_verification_(§12.2)', 'recruiter_hub_(§18.3)'],
  certification_authority: ['certification_verification_(§12.2)', 'bulk_record_ingestion_(§16.3)'],
  // Every §4.2 OrgType gets a row; unlisted types fall back to the base set every
  // organisation gets regardless of sector: identity verification requests, staff/
  // affiliation management (§42), and messaging.
};
// Enforced the same way role-based nav gating already works (§10.4 avatar dropdown,
// §16.6 country adapters) — a visibility filter on one shared module tree, checked
// alongside auth and onboarding in the same ROUTE_GUARD pass above, not a separate check.
```

## 35. ACTIVATION AUDIT REQUIREMENTS

```
AUDIT RULE: If users can see it, they must be able to use it.
No dead clicks. No empty buttons. No static placeholders.
Every element that exists must function fully.

IDENTIFY AND ACTIVATE ALL:
✓ Static buttons        → loading state + outcome feedback
✓ Non-functional tabs   → content loaded + state preserved
✓ Placeholder components → real content or purposeful empty states
✓ Unfinished forms      → validate + submit + provide feedback
✓ Inactive modals       → open + perform action + close correctly
✓ Disconnected pages    → integrate with routing and state
✓ Broken state          → persist across navigation and refresh

EMPTY STATE RULE:
Every page and section: purposeful empty state (NEVER blank)
Format: explanation of what's missing + clear actionable CTA
Example: "You haven't recorded any activities yet.
          Record your first activity to start building your professional identity."
         [Record Your First Activity →]
```

### 35.1 Eight Pillars — Classification Lens (audit metadata only)

```
Every feature audited under this section must additionally be tagged with the Ecosystem
Loop pillar(s) it serves: Learning · Identity · Evidence · Verification · Trust ·
Opportunity · Contribution · Prosperity (extends §1.4's Immutable System Law and the
Learning→...→Prosperity framing already cited at §10.1/§50.9.0).

This is documentation/audit metadata only — it adds no new field to Activity, User, or
any other schema. Health Dashboards (§50.9.3) and Career Timeline (§50.9.6) already
capture the same signal in schema-backed form; this checklist exists so a feature with
no pillar it can honestly be tagged against gets flagged for removal or redesign before
ship, per Patch Law 3's "ecosystem-aligned only" rule — not so pillars get tracked twice.
```

---

## 36. FINAL IMPLEMENTATION RULES

```
1.  DO NOT REMOVE any feature from any previous patch or existing MVP
2.  DO NOT SIMPLIFY the vision or architecture
3.  DO NOT REBUILD from scratch — patch, fix, activate, and elevate
4.  DO NOT ADD competitive comparisons to any other platform
5.  HARMONISE overlapping features into unified hubs — no functionality lost
6.  ACTIVATE every visible element with real functionality
7.  PRESERVE both pathways (Academic + Competency) at equal weight in ALL
    ranking, discovery, and matching — always
8.  ENFORCE authenticity through the Organisation Mention System and Trust Center,
    NOT through onboarding gates
9.  KEEP registration fast — no verification documents, no institutional numbers
    at sign-up, ever
10. MAKE the Trust Center the most powerful credibility engine ever built
    on a professional platform
11. ENSURE all domains (profileos.com, getprofileos.com, myprofileos.com,
    profiletrust.com, builtonproof.com) are fully synched and routed
12. EVERY route must work. Every navigation path must resolve.
    Every CTA must connect to its destination.
13. DARK MODE and LIGHT MODE must work perfectly across every screen,
    every component, and every state
14. AI-generated CVs and Portfolios must ALWAYS remain fully editable by the user
15. PROFILE must feel less like a website and more like the global operating
    system for professional truth
```

## OPEN ITEMS FOR PRODUCT DECISION

```
1. COMMUNITIES — listed as discovery category — needs own data model,
   membership rules, and verification path before build.
   Recommend: out of scope for v1 until defined.

RESOLVED: Item 2, TRUST SCORE COEFFICIENTS — §13.2 already ships numerically
specified launch weights (TRUST_WEIGHTS: identity_verification 25, employment_verification
25, academic_degree_verification 20, etc.), and §50.1's TRUST_GOVERNANCE_POLICY already
defines who may change them (compliance_officer + super_admin, dual sign-off), the review
cadence (quarterly or after a confirmed dispute pattern), and the required bias/fairness
review before any change ships. §13.2's weights are the v1 launch baseline, set by domain
judgement rather than a labelled dataset (none exists pre-launch), with mandatory
recalibration against real dispute and outcome data no later than the first §50.1
quarterly review. Every recalibration is captured by the existing
TrustScoreFormulaChangeLog (§50.1), so historical scores remain reconstructable under the
formula active when calculated (§13.3) even as weights are tuned post-launch.

RESOLVED: Item 3, DISPUTE RESOLUTION — §50.1 already defines a full Dispute schema
(disputeType includes 'verification_decision', 'trust_score_change', and
'recruiter_filter_exclusion'; status lifecycle 'submitted' → 'under_review' → 'upheld' /
'corrected' / 'removed' / 'escalated' / 'independent_review'; 14-day appeal window) plus
named entry points (DISPUTE_ENTRY_POINTS) surfaced directly on the verification-outcome
screen, major trust-score-change notifications, and recruiter-filter-exclusion
explanations. The process this item asked for is §50.1's Dispute + DISPUTE_ENTRY_POINTS,
extending §28.4's Enforcement Escalation for the trust/verification/recruiter-filter cases
§28.4 does not itself cover.

4. DATA RETENTION & PORTABILITY — Trust Passport data rules for users who leave
   the platform need legal review (cross-border academic + recruiter use cases).

5. TOOL PROVIDER AGREEMENTS — commercial and legal framework needed before any
   provider-facing usage signal is built (even in anonymised form).

6. ADMIN-REQUIRES-PRIOR-AFFILIATION (§42.3 Rule 6) — confirm this doesn't block
   any admin invite flow for brand-new organisations with no staff roster yet
   (e.g. a just-registered company inviting its first HR Manager).

7. WORKSPACE SWITCHER UX (§10.3/10.4) — needs design sign-off on the switcher
   pattern before build.

8. NO-ANONYMOUS-ORGANISATION-CREATION (§8.4.1) — confirm this prerequisite is
   acceptable for all organisation-creation flows.

9. "VERIFIED AFFILIATION ENGINE" NAMING (§4.2/§42) — confirm whether this
   external/marketing term should be used for what is implemented here as an
   extension of the existing Organisation schema, VXI, and §42 admin roles, or
   whether that naming should be dropped in favour of "People & Affiliations."

RESOLVED: Item 10, NAMED GROWTH SURFACES — Graduate Launchpad, Student
Opportunity Hub, and Professional Growth Journey are promoted to dedicated sub-routes
(/opportunity/students, /opportunity/graduates, /career-growth/journey) per §18.5 and
§50.9.6, rather than left as unrouted filtered views or new nav-level tabs.
```

## CORE GOVERNING PRINCIPLES — SECTIONS 37–50

```
PRINCIPLE 1 — ADDITIVE ARCHITECTURE
Every rule below adds to the PROFILE architecture defined in Sections 1–36.
Nothing there is removed, redesigned, or replaced.

PRINCIPLE 2 — BACKWARD COMPATIBLE
Every schema field introduced below is optional unless explicitly marked required.
No workflow below may break an existing workflow.

PRINCIPLE 3 — ECOSYSTEM-ALIGNED ONLY
Every feature must trace back to the Ecosystem Loop:
Identity → Evidence → Verification → Trust → Discovery → Opportunity → Growth
Features that only drive engagement, popularity, or vanity metrics are rejected on sight.

PRINCIPLE 4 — SECURITY BEFORE FEATURE
No monetization, marketplace, or discovery feature may ship ahead of the security
and governance controls defined in Section 37.

PRINCIPLE 5 — SINGLE ROUTING HOME
Every feature belongs to exactly one primary module (§50.7 Feature Routing Matrix).
A feature may inherit behavior from supporting modules, but is never implemented
twice in two places. Where a genuine conflict exists between requirements, the
currently-specified implementation below wins.
```

# 37. SECURITY, TRUST & GOVERNANCE HARDENING

### 37.0 Scope

This section is a full security, privacy, compliance, and resilience audit layer applied
on top of §27 (Security Architecture) and §28 (Trust Governance). It does not replace
either section — it hardens them.

### 37.1 Full-Surface Security Audit Scope

```yaml
audit_targets:
  - authentication
  - authorization
  - database_permissions
  - api_endpoints
  - file_uploads
  - media_storage
  - credential_storage
  - verification_workflows
  - messaging
  - notifications
  - session_handling
  - cookies
  - local_storage
  - tokens
  - environment_variables
  - admin_permissions
standard: enterprise_security_best_practice
frequency: pre_deployment + quarterly_recurring
```

### 37.2 Password Security & Breach Detection

```typescript
interface PasswordSecurityPolicy {
  hashing: 'Argon2id' | 'bcrypt';        // Argon2id preferred; bcrypt with cost ≥ 12 as fallback
  breachCheck: {
    provider: 'HaveIBeenPwned Password API';
    method: 'k-anonymity';                // only a hash prefix is ever transmitted
    neverTransmitPlaintext: true;
    blockRegistrationOnMatch: true;
    blockPasswordChangeOnMatch: true;
    userMessaging: 'clear_non_shaming_guidance_to_choose_a_new_password';
  };
  policy: {
    minLength: 8;
    requires: ['uppercase', 'lowercase', 'number', 'special_character'];
    strengthMeter: true;
    reusePrevention: 'last_5_password_hashes';
  };
}
// HARD RULE: passwords are never logged, never emailed, never stored reversibly.
```

### 37.3 Multi-Factor Authentication

```typescript
const MFA_METHODS = [
  'authenticator_app_TOTP',
  'email_verification',
  'sms_optional',
  'passkeys_webauthn',                  // live — was 'future_ready'; §50.2 already mandates
                                          // it for 3 of 4 risk tiers, see §37.26.1
  'backup_recovery_codes',
  'hardware_security_key',              // §37.26.1
  'biometric_platform_authenticator',    // §37.26.1
  'login_approval_push',                 // §37.26.1
];

interface TrustedDeviceManagement {
  viewTrustedDevices: true;
  revokeDeviceInstantly: true;
  notifyOnNewDeviceLogin: true;
}
```

### 37.4 Database Security

```sql
-- Example remediation pattern for functions with mutable search paths
-- (applies to every SECURITY DEFINER / SECURITY INVOKER function in the schema)
ALTER FUNCTION public.update_usage()
  SET search_path = public, pg_temp;

-- Rule: every function must declare an explicit, minimal search_path.
-- Rule: SECURITY DEFINER is used only where privilege elevation is required
--       and documented; SECURITY INVOKER is the default.
-- Rule: no function may be left with an implicit/mutable search path.
```

### 37.5 Row Level Security (RLS) Policy Matrix

```typescript
const RLS_HARD_RULE = 'No table may ship with an "always true" or unrestricted policy.';

const RLS_ACCESS_MODEL = [
  { actor: 'Individual User',        access: 'own records only' },
  { actor: 'Organisation',           access: 'organisation-owned resources only' },
  { actor: 'Institutional Verifier', access: 'assigned verification queues only' },
  { actor: 'Recruiter',              access: 'candidate data explicitly shared with them only' },
  { actor: 'Administrator',          access: 'role-scoped, fully audit-logged' },
];
// Every table requires a documented, intentional RLS policy before it ships.
// "No policy defined" is treated as a security defect, not a default-safe state.
```

### 37.6 Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)

```typescript
type PlatformRole =
  | 'individual_user'
  | 'organisation'
  | 'company'
  | 'university'
  | 'government_agency'
  | 'verification_authority'
  | 'recruiter'
  | 'recruitment_partner'          // see §39.3
  | 'moderator'
  | 'customer_support'
  | 'compliance_officer'
  | 'super_administrator';

interface RolePermission {
  role: PlatformRole;
  permissions: string[];           // explicit allow-list — never inferred
  canEscalate: false;               // no role may self-escalate privileges
}
```

### 37.7 Audit Logging (extends §4 System fields)

```typescript
interface SecurityAuditEntry {
  id:          string;
  actorId:     string;
  action:
    | 'login' | 'logout' | 'password_change' | 'mfa_change'
    | 'email_change' | 'phone_change' | 'trust_score_change'
    | 'credential_upload' | 'credential_verification' | 'verification_decision'
    | 'profile_change' | 'privacy_setting_change' | 'admin_action';
  target?:     string;
  ipAddress:   string;
  deviceFingerprint: string;
  timestamp:   Date;
  immutable:   true;
  retention:   'per_compliance_policy';
}
// Logs are searchable by admins with appropriate role, never editable, never deletable
// except under documented legal/compliance retention expiry.
```
