# 45. FAQ LIBRARY

### 45.0 Routing

Canonical content for `/help/faq`, linked from Help Centre (§37.10), Footer, and onboarding.

```
1.  What is PROFILE, exactly?
    PROFILE is a professional identity platform where your work, education, and credentials
    get verified by the institutions and employers behind them — not just claimed by you.

2.  How is this different from LinkedIn or a resume builder?
    Resume builders format whatever you type; they don't check if it's true. PROFILE routes
    credentials to the actual university, employer, or licensing body for confirmation.

3.  Who is PROFILE for?
    Individuals building a verified career record, organisations hiring or verifying staff,
    and institutions confirming credentials at scale — all inside the same system.

4.  Is PROFILE free to use?
    Yes — building your profile, recording work, and requesting verification are free.
    Paid plans add CV downloads, priority verification, and recruiter-level discovery.

5.  What do I get for upgrading?
    The free plan proves your work is real; premium adds visibility and convenience —
    PDF export, higher search ranking, multiple CV templates. Verification is never paywalled.

6.  How do I know this is legitimate?
    Every verification routes directly to the issuing institution or employer, not to us.
    You can see who verified each item, when, and exactly how it affected your trust score.

7.  What happens if a verification request is rejected?
    You'll see the rejection reason and can appeal or submit more evidence on the same
    request — nothing is silently dropped.

8.  What if my university or employer isn't on PROFILE?
    We invite them to join and respond. Meanwhile your credential stays visible, marked
    unverified, so nothing you added disappears while you wait.

9.  Is my data safe?
    Documents are encrypted, you control exactly who sees what, and we never sell data
    to advertisers (see §46 Privacy & Data Policy).

10. What happens right after I sign up?
    You create an account with just name, phone/email, and a password — no documents
    required. You build your profile, then head into the Trust Center when ready to verify.

11. How long does verification take?
    Most requests resolve in 2–5 business days, with a live status from submitted to verified.

12. How is my Trust Score calculated?
    Entirely from verified evidence — confirmed employment, degrees, licences, reviews —
    with every point traceable to its source (e.g. "+25 Employment Verified by [Company]").

13. Does PROFILE build my CV for me?
    Yes — auto-generated from verified activity and always fully editable by you (individual
    accounts only; organisation accounts get a Portfolio, not a CV — see §37.13/§43).

14. Do employers actually hire through PROFILE?
    Recruiters search by verified skill, trust tier, and confirmed experience, and candidates
    apply with a verified profile instead of a separate CV upload.

15. Can organisations post jobs and manage applicants?
    Yes — a full Hiring Dashboard with pipeline, comparison, and trust-based filtering (§39.3).

16. What can government agencies or licensing bodies do here?
    Define verification schemas, confirm licences at scale, and issue verifiable digital
    badges tied to their authority (§16, §38.13).

17. Is there a mobile app?
    PROFILE installs on mobile and desktop; core features work offline and sync on reconnect.

18. Can I take my data with me if I leave?
    Yes — export your CV and credential history anytime; deletion follows our Privacy &
    Data Policy (§46.4). Verified records tied to institutions remain yours to share, always.

If your question isn't answered here, reach out to us directly and we'll get you a straight answer.
```

# 46. LEGAL & POLICY DOCUMENT LIBRARY

### 46.0 Status Note

The drafts below are founder-facing starting copy for `/about`, `/trust`, `/privacy`, `/terms`,
and the five consent documents referenced throughout the Master Prompt (§8.3, §31). They are
written in PROFILE's brand voice (§44) with a plain-language summary above each formal section.
**These require review by qualified legal counsel in each operating jurisdiction before
publication** — this is standard practice for any platform handling identity documents and
credential data, not a gap in this patch.

### 46.1 About / Trust / Privacy / Terms — Footer Page Map

```typescript
const FOOTER_LEGAL_ROUTES = {
  '/about':   'Full narrative — see §43',
  '/trust':   'Public Trust Principles overview — see §46.2',
  '/privacy': 'Privacy & Data Policy — see §46.4',
  '/terms':   'Terms of Service — see §46.6',
};
```

### 46.2 Trust Principles (Public Overview)

> **In plain terms:** we don't rank you by popularity, we tell you exactly why your trust
> score changed, and no verification badge appears unless the issuing institution confirmed it.

1. **Evidence over claims.** Nothing is presented as verified unless a named institution, employer, or authority confirmed it.
2. **Transparency by default.** Every trust score change cites its source (§13.3).
3. **No vanity metrics.** Followers, likes, and views never factor into ranking or trust (§17.1).
4. **User-controlled visibility.** You decide what's public, what's shared per-link, and what stays private (§12.6).
5. **Right to appeal.** Every verification decision can be appealed within 14 days (§28.4).

### 46.3 1 — Identity & Authenticity Agreement

> **In plain terms:** the name, documents, and history you give us have to be genuinely yours.

By creating a PROFILE account, you agree that: (a) all information you submit is accurate and belongs to you; (b) you will not create multiple accounts to misrepresent separate identities; (c) any credential submitted for verification is one you are legitimately entitled to hold; (d) PROFILE may suspend or revoke access if impersonation, identity fraud, or credential forgery is confirmed; (e) your Profile Identity ID (PID) is permanent, non-transferable, and never reused, even after account closure. This agreement is versioned and re-presented for acceptance whenever materially changed (30 days' notice per §27.4).

### 46.4 4 — Privacy & Data Policy

> **In plain terms:** your documents are encrypted, you control who sees what, and we never sell your data.

**What we collect:** account details you provide, credentials and documents you upload for verification, activity you record, and usage data needed to operate the platform securely.
**How it's protected:** AES-256 encryption at rest, TLS 1.3 in transit, role-based access controls, and full audit logging on every access to sensitive records (§37).
**How it's shared:** only with the specific institution or employer you're requesting verification from, or with parties you explicitly grant access to via visibility controls (§12.6). We do not sell personal data to advertisers.
**Your rights:** access, export, correction, deletion, and consent withdrawal, consistent with GDPR, NDPR, and CCPA (§27.4). Requests are handled by the Compliance Officer role (§42.2) within statutory timeframes.
**Retention:** verification and audit records are retained per regulatory requirement even after account deletion where legally required; all other data is deleted upon request.

### 46.5 2 — Verification Routing Consent

> **In plain terms:** when you request verification, we send your details to the specific institution you named — not anyone else.

By submitting a verification request, you consent to PROFILE transmitting the identifiers and documents you provide (§12.3 UVIF) to the named institution, employer, or authority solely for the purpose of confirming that specific credential. You may withdraw a pending request at any time before a decision is issued. Approved verifications are recorded permanently in your Credential Vault (§12.5) as part of your auditable trust history; withdrawal after approval requires a formal revocation request reviewed by a Compliance Officer.

### 46.6 Terms of Service

> **In plain terms:** use PROFILE honestly, don't misrepresent your work or credentials, and we'll keep the platform running fairly for everyone.

**Eligibility:** minimum age 15 to hold an account, 18 to transact on the Marketplace (§28.5).
**Account responsibilities:** keep credentials confidential, report suspected compromise immediately, and maintain accurate profile information per §46.3.
**Platform conduct:** the prohibited-conduct list in §28.2–28.3 of the Master Prompt applies in full (impersonation, fraud, harassment, review manipulation, and related conduct).
**Fees and subscriptions:** free-tier access continues indefinitely; paid tiers are billed as disclosed at purchase and may be cancelled at any time, with access continuing through the paid period.
**Termination:** PROFILE may suspend or terminate accounts for confirmed violations following the escalation levels in §28.4, with appeal rights except where child-safety violations are involved (§28.5).
**Changes to these terms:** material changes require 30 days' notice and re-acceptance (§27.4).

### 46.7 3 — Professional Conduct Standards

> **In plain terms:** treat other users, institutions, and organisations the way you'd want to be treated in a professional setting.

Users must not: impersonate individuals or organisations; falsify work history, credentials, or affiliations; participate in verification rings or review-exchange networks; harass, threaten, or discriminate against other users; use bot-generated engagement or synthetic identities; upload others' work to the Copyright system as their own. Verified badges and trust scores may be revoked for confirmed violations, following the same enforcement escalation defined in §28.4.

### 46.8 5 — Data Processing Agreement (DPA)

> **In plain terms:** this is the formal agreement institutional and enterprise customers sign when PROFILE processes personal data on their behalf.

Applies to institution, government, and enterprise accounts using bulk verification, API access, or white-label infrastructure (§38.9, §38.18). Defines PROFILE as **data processor** and the institution as **data controller** for records the institution submits for verification purposes. Covers: processing scope limited to verification and credential-management purposes only; sub-processor disclosure (hosting, encryption, backup providers); breach notification within 72 hours of confirmed incident; data return or deletion on contract termination; audit rights for the controller consistent with §27.4 compliance obligations. Executed as a standalone signed agreement alongside the institution's subscription contract — this document defines its required contents, not a substitute for legal execution.

# 47. PROFESSIONAL CAREER ENGINE — CV, PORTFOLIO & COVER LETTER

### 47.0 Positioning Correction (extends §15)

Master Prompt §15 already defines a CV/Portfolio Auto-Build Engine. This section does not
replace it — it upgrades the positioning and closes real schema gaps. PROFILE does not ship
"a CV builder." LinkedIn has profiles, Canva has CV templates, Resume.io has resume builders.
PROFILE ships a **Professional Career Engine**: three interconnected, continuously-evolving
living documents, all fed from the same verified evidence base.

```
PROFILE CV            — career-focused, recruiter- and ATS-facing
PROFILE Portfolio      — evidence-focused, proof-of-work facing
PROFILE Cover Letter   — opportunity-specific, generated per application
```

None of these are static PDFs. All three update automatically as verified credentials,
projects, skills, activity, and evidence are added — this is the same Auto-Update Trigger
mechanism already defined in §5.3, extended to a third document type.

### 47.1 Career Engine Principles

```typescript
const CAREER_ENGINE_PRINCIPLES = [
  'build_from_verified_evidence_wherever_possible',
  'allow_manual_addition_where_no_evidence_exists',
  'preserve_full_version_history',
  'support_multiple_versions_per_industry_or_goal',
  'always_editable_without_losing_verified_data',
  'ats_compliant_output',
  'multilingual_content_support',
];
// Restates and extends the §15.1 Core Principle: AI assists, the user always owns and edits.
```

### 47.2 PROFILE CV — Schema Extension (extends §4.6 CVDocument)

```typescript
interface CVDocumentV3 extends CVDocument {
  // New fields not present in §4.6
  languages:        LanguageProficiency[];
  awards:            CVSection<AwardEntry[]>;
  volunteerExperience: CVSection<VolunteerEntry[]>;
  professionalMemberships: CVSection<MembershipEntry[]>;
  references:        CVSection<ReferenceEntry[]>;   // distinct from clientReviews — peer/professional references
  exportFormats:     ('pdf' | 'docx' | 'html')[];    // §4.6 previously implied PDF-only (premium)
  qrCode:            string;                          // already present in §4.6 — reaffirmed here
}
// exportFormats: PDF export remains premium per §15.3; DOCX and HTML export are new,
// included at the free tier to maximise ATS compatibility without a paywall on basic usability.
```

### 47.3 PROFILE Portfolio — Item-Level Extension (extends §4.6 portfolio section, §4.3 Activity)

```typescript
// Activity already supports includeInCV / includeInPortfolio booleans (§4.3).
// This extends portfolio ITEMS (not just activities) with the same toggle pattern,
// covering directly-uploaded evidence that didn't originate from an Activity post.

interface PortfolioItemV3 extends PortfolioItem {
  itemType:
    | 'project' | 'design' | 'case_study' | 'report' | 'research' | 'article'
    | 'image' | 'video' | 'audio' | 'presentation' | 'git_repository'
    | 'publication' | 'client_work' | 'testimonial' | 'award' | 'patent';
  addToCV:          boolean;
  addToPortfolio:    boolean;   // addToBoth = both flags true; no separate enum needed
  skillsUsed:        Skill[];
  toolsUsed:         Tool[];
  selfTag:           boolean;
  collaboratorAttribution: Collaborator[];
  organisationAttribution: string[];
  institutionAttribution:  string[];
  timelinePlacement: Date;
  verificationStatus: VerificationStatus;
  downloadPermission: 'anyone' | 'connections' | 'recruiters' | 'owner_only';
  visibility:        'public' | 'connections' | 'recruiters' | 'verified_orgs' | 'private';
}
```

### 47.4 PROFILE Cover Letter — New Schema

```typescript
interface CoverLetterDocument {
  id:             string;
  userId:         string;
  title:          string;                 // e.g. "General" or "Flutterwave — Product Designer"
  targetOrganisation?: string;
  targetRole?:    string;
  linkedOpportunityId?: string;           // ties to JobListing (§4.9) when generated for a specific apply action
  linkedCVVersion: number;                // which CVDocument.version it was generated alongside
  body:           string;
  tone:           'formal' | 'conversational' | 'technical' | 'academic';
  isAIAssisted:   boolean;
  isEditable:     true;                   // always — same non-negotiable rule as §15.1
  exportFormats:  ('pdf' | 'docx')[];
  version:        number;
  createdAt:      Date;
  updatedAt:      Date;
}
// HARD RULE: the Cover Letter Engine may only reorganise and phrase verified or
// user-provided information. It may never invent experience, dates, or qualifications
// not already present in the user's PROFILE (same rule as §48.0 AI Principles).
```

### 47.5 CV Template Library (extends §15.3 — templates: 10)

```typescript
const CV_TEMPLATE_LIBRARY = [
  'Executive', 'Corporate', 'Modern Professional', 'Minimalist', 'Creative',
  'Academic & Research', 'Technical & Engineering', 'Healthcare',
  'Government & Public Service', 'International Professional (Europass-inspired)',
];
// Requirements: ATS-compliant, print-ready, mobile-friendly, accessible,
// light/dark preview modes, switchable without losing content — extends §15.3 CV_INTERFACE.
```

```typescript
  legalMiddleName?: string;
```

### 47.9 Sensitive Identifier Visibility Rule

```typescript
// Resolves the open item on User ID / Copyright / Trust Passport number exposure.
const SENSITIVE_IDENTIFIER_TYPES = [
  'profile_id_pid',            // §9.2
  'copyright_id',              // §11.4, format #PRF-YYYY-XXXX
  'trust_passport_number',     // §12.6
  'verification_reference_numbers', // §12.4
];

interface SensitiveIdentifierVisibility {
  identifierType: string;
  hiddenByDefault: true;              // always — no exceptions
  visibleTo:       'owner_only';      // default state
  ownerCanToggle:  boolean;           // owner may explicitly enable per-identifier sharing
  sharedContext:   'specific_verification_flow' | 'specific_share_link' | 'never';
  neverIndexedPublicly: true;         // excluded from SEO metadata (§47.10) and public HTML unconditionally
}
// HARD RULE: even when an owner enables sharing, a sensitive identifier is shown only
// inside the specific context that required it (e.g. an institution's verification
// review screen) — never rendered into a public profile page or search-indexable content.
```

```typescript
  tools:         Tool[];

}
// HARD RULE: SEO metadata is generated only from content the user has already
```


### 47.12 Privacy & Access Control Matrix

| Permission | Private | Public | Connections Only | Recruiters Only | Verified Orgs Only | Custom |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| View | owner only | anyone | connections | recruiters | verified orgs | per-grant |
| Download | – | if enabled | if enabled | if enabled | if enabled | per-grant |
| Print | – | if enabled | if enabled | if enabled | if enabled | per-grant |
| Share | – | if enabled | if enabled | if enabled | if enabled | per-grant |
| Export | owner only | owner only | owner only | owner only | owner only | owner only |

```
RULE: Export (generating a downloadable PDF/DOCX file) is always owner-initiated.
Third parties may be granted Download of an owner-exported file, never independent
export rights over someone else's Career Engine documents.
```

### 47.13 Creator Hub

A dedicated, specialised **view** over the Portfolio Engine (§47.3) and Trust record for
writers, researchers, designers, filmmakers, photographers, musicians, architects,
developers, engineers, and consultants — deliberately **not** a second, parallel profile
system. Everything the Creator Hub shows already exists as Portfolio items, Activity
evidence (§4.3), and Copyright/Proof-of-Creation records (§11.4); the Hub is a
presentation and discovery layer combining portfolio management, project showcases,
client discovery, collaboration entry points, and Creator Commerce (§38.20) listings in
one place, with zero duplicated data.

```typescript
interface CreatorHubProfile {
  userId: string;
  featuredPortfolioItemIds: string[];   // subset of §47.3 PortfolioItemV3 records
  creatorCategories: string[];           // e.g. 'writer', 'photographer', 'developer'
  commerceListingIds: string[];          // §38.20 Creator Commerce listings, if any
  clientDiscoverable: boolean;           // opt-in — surfaces in Discover (§17) as a creator
}
```

# 48. PROFILE AI ENGINE

### 48.0 AI Principles (Hard Rules — apply platform-wide)

```typescript
const AI_ENGINE_PRINCIPLES = [
  'ai_may_generate_organise_and_suggest',
  'ai_may_never_fabricate_experience_qualifications_or_achievements',
  'ai_may_organise_recommend_summarise_and_infer_but_must_never_convert_an_inference_into_a_verified_fact',
                                    // e.g. AI may suggest "this looks like Adobe Premiere" from an
                                    // upload's metadata, but that becomes a tagged skill/tool only once
                                    // the user confirms it — it is never auto-written as verified evidence
  'user_remains_owner_and_final_editor_of_every_ai_output',       // restates §15.1
  'ai_generated_content_is_always_labelled_as_such',
  'ai_never_bypasses_verification_ai_cannot_verify_a_credential',  // verification is institutional, not algorithmic
];
// This section consolidates every AI touchpoint already implied elsewhere in the
// architecture (§16.4 AI-Assisted Matching, §38.17 AI Services, Career Engine §47)
// into one governed subsystem so no AI feature ships without passing these rules.
```

### 48.0a PROFILE Copilot™ — The Unified Intelligence Brand (names and organises §48's
existing capabilities under one product identity; introduces no new AI capability of its own)

```
POSITIONING: everything in §48 — AI-assisted verification matching (§48.2), discovery and
opportunity ranking (§48.3), trust and fraud detection (§48.4), AI Introductions (§48.8),
the Collaborator Hub (§48.9), PROFILE Advisor (§48.10), and the provider-independent AI
Gateway (§48.11) — is, collectively, ONE product with one name: PROFILE Copilot™. A user
never encounters "the AI Engine" as a labelled thing; they encounter PROFILE Copilot,
present in different forms depending on where they are in the product. This subsection is
the naming and organisational layer over §48.1–§48.11 — it does not add a second AI system,
a second Hard Rule set (§48.0 governs Copilot exactly as it already governs the AI Engine),
or a second provider-routing mechanism (§48.11's AI Gateway is Copilot's engine room,
unchanged).

PROFILE Copilot is not a chatbot, not a search engine, and not a replacement for human
decision-making. It is a trusted professional companion that turns data into guidance,
evidence into trust, and trust into opportunity — the same Ecosystem Loop (§1.4) as
everything else in this document, applied to an AI layer instead of a feature.
```

```typescript
// CORE PERSONALITY — governs tone and register for every Copilot-generated string,
// platform-wide. Extends §44 Brand Voice (Grounded, Exact, Composed, Human) with the
// additional constraints specific to an AI speaking in first person about a user's own
// evidence and trust standing.
const COPILOT_PERSONALITY_TRAITS = [
  'professional', 'trustworthy', 'calm', 'patient', 'natural', 'respectful',
  'transparent', 'honest', 'evidence_driven',
];
const COPILOT_PERSONALITY_HARD_RULES = [
  'never_arrogant', 'never_exaggerated', 'never_manipulative', 'never_overly_verbose',
  'never_claims_confidence_in_something_it_cannot_verify',
  'always_distinguishes_facts_from_assumptions_from_recommendations',   // restates the
                                    // existing platform-wide epistemic discipline this
                                    // document already requires of Claude-authored content
                                    // in the userPreferences sense — now codified as a
                                    // product requirement for PROFILE's own AI
];

// UNIVERSAL PRESENCE — Copilot is context-aware everywhere in the product, not a
// destination page. This is the context object every Copilot surface reads from; it
// introduces no new data collection — every field is already tracked elsewhere in this
// document (career stage: §50.9.1; trust level: §13; profile completeness: §50.9.3;
// permissions: §37.6; language/country: §30) and is simply assembled into one context view.
interface CopilotContext {
  currentPage:        string;
  userType:            'individual' | 'organisation' | 'institution' | 'government' | 'recruiter';
  goals:               string[];              // §8.7 ONBOARDING_GOALS, editable in Settings
  permissions:         string[];               // §37.6 RBAC — Copilot never acts outside them
  trustLevel:          TrustTier;               // §13.1
  profileCompleteness: ProfileHealthScores;      // §50.9.3
  careerStage:         CareerStage;              // §50.9.1
  institution?:        string;
  industry?:           string;
  language:            string;                   // §30
  country:             string;
  currentActivity?:    string;
  previousInteractions: string[];                // recent Copilot interaction refs, capped
                                                  // and user-clearable — never a hidden,
                                                  // unbounded behavioural log
}
```

```typescript
// COPILOT SKILLS FRAMEWORK — PROFILE Copilot is architected as modular skills sharing one
// context object (CopilotContext above) and one Hard Rule set (§48.0), not one monolithic
// assistant. Every skill below already exists as a capability somewhere in this document;
// this table is the routing layer that lets a user experience them as one coherent
// companion instead of sixteen unrelated features. No skill listed below introduces new
// functionality beyond what its governing section already specifies.
const COPILOT_SKILLS: Record<string, string> = {
  'Identity Copilot':      '§9 Global Identity Resolution, §9.7 Private Professional Mode guidance',
  'CV Copilot':            '§47.1–47.2, §48.1 AI Career Assistant',
  'Portfolio Copilot':     '§47.3, §48.1',
  'Career Copilot':        '§48.10/§50.9.2 PROFILE Advisor — this is PROFILE Advisor\'s
                             role within the Copilot Skills Framework; the established
                             name "PROFILE Advisor" is retained for this skill specifically
                             rather than renamed, since it is already the free, always-on,
                             user-facing name referenced throughout §11.0, §18.5, §39.13,
                             and §50.9 — Career Copilot is what it is called in this table,
                             PROFILE Advisor is what a user sees',
  'Job Copilot':           '§48.3 AI in Discovery & Opportunity Matching, §18',
  'Recruiter Copilot':     '§18.3 Recruiter Mode, §39.4 Candidate Matching Engine',
  'Trust Copilot':         '§13.3 Transparency Rules, §48.4 AI in Trust & Fraud Detection',
  'Verification Copilot':  '§48.2 AI in Verification Matching, §16.4',
  'Learning Copilot':      '§21 Learning & Communities, §50.9.7 PROFILE Academy',
  'Marketplace Copilot':   '§38.1–38.3 Professional Services, Promotion, Ads',
  'Research Copilot':      '§47.13 Creator Hub, §50.9.5\'s Researcher row',
  'Institution Copilot':   '§16.5 Institutional Review Console, §50.4 IOTR',
  'Analytics Copilot':     '§23, §40.9 Analytics Evolution',
  'Security Copilot':      '§37.26.11 — the Security & Trust Center\'s guidance role',
  'API / Developer Copilot': '§16.7, §38.10 — API documentation, SDK examples, integration guidance',
  'Enterprise Copilot':    '§38.18 Enterprise Solutions',
};
// RULE: a new AI capability is added to PROFILE by extending the governing section for its
// domain and, if user-facing, adding one row here — never by building a seventeenth,
// unrouted skill or a second personality/context model.
```

### 48.1 AI Career Assistant (extends §47 Career Engine)

```typescript
const AI_CAREER_ASSISTANT_CAPABILITIES = [
  'build_a_cv_from_raw_information', 'review_cv_for_ats_compatibility',
  'rewrite_bullet_points_with_stronger_action_verbs', 'tailor_cv_to_a_specific_role',
  'generate_career_change_cvs', 'generate_graduate_cvs',
  'produce_role_specific_cover_letters', 'analyse_job_descriptions',
  'extract_keywords', 'suggest_missing_skills', 'recommend_certifications',
  'identify_portfolio_gaps', 'improve_interview_readiness',
];
// Bound by §48.0: enhances and reorganises verified or user-provided information only.
```

### 48.2 AI In Verification Matching (extends §16.4 Matching Stages)

```
Reaffirms the existing AI-Assisted Matching engine: handles spelling variance,
nicknames, transliteration, and regional naming when matching a submitted
credential against an institution's dataset (e.g. Mohammed / Muhammad / Mohamad).
This patch adds no new matching logic — it formally brings that engine under
the AI Engine governance rules in §48.0, including mandatory confidence-score
transparency (§16.4 Stage 3) and human review for anything below the auto-verify threshold.
```

### 48.3 AI In Discovery & Opportunity Matching (extends §17.3, §18.4, §40.4)

```
Semantic search ranking (§17.3 Smart Search) and opportunity/academic matching
(§18.4, §40.7) may use AI for relevance and matching quality. AI ranking still
inherits the platform-wide Hard Rule (§17.1, §40.4): trust and verification
signals are the ranking inputs, never popularity or engagement metrics —
AI is a relevance layer on top of that ranking, not a replacement for it.
```

### 48.4 AI In Trust & Fraud Detection (extends §27.3 Active Defence)

```typescript
const AI_TRUST_SAFETY_FUNCTIONS = [
  'anomaly_detection_behavioural_analytics',   // already in §27.3
  'duplicate_upload_and_content_hash_matching', // already in §11.4
  'profile_photo_and_credential_similarity_scanning', // already in §9.5 Anti-Impersonation
  'ai_shortlisting_recommendations_for_recruiters',   // already in §18.3
];
// This section does not add new fraud-detection capability; it formally routes the
// AI-driven parts of §9.5, §11.4, §18.3, and §27.3 through the same governance layer.
```

### 48.5 AI Transparency & Disclosure Rule

```typescript
interface AIDisclosureRequirement {
  aiGeneratedCVDraft:        'labelled_as_AI_drafted_until_user_edits';
  aiGeneratedCoverLetter:    'labelled_as_AI_assisted';
  aiRankedRecruiterResults:  'labelled_as_AI_ranked_by_trust_and_relevance_signals';
  aiFraudFlag:               'never_shown_publicly_routed_to_moderation_only';
}
// HARD RULE: no AI-generated content is ever presented to a third party (recruiter,
// institution, public visitor) without disclosure that AI assisted its creation,
// consistent with the "AI is an assistant, the user is the owner" rule in §15.1.
```

### 48.6 AI Data Governance

```
User data is never used to train third-party foundation models without explicit,
separately-scoped consent. Any AI processing of personal or credential data
inherits the encryption, access-control, and audit-logging requirements of §37,
and any AI vendor relationship is subject to the same Data Processing Agreement
structure defined in §46.8.
```

| AI Career Assistant (rewrite, tailor, ATS review) | Live | §48.1 |

### 48.8 AI Professional Introductions (extends §17.3 Smart Search, §40.5 PROFILE References)

Replaces passive "people you may know" surfaces with contextual, evidence-cited
introductions: *"You and Sarah both worked on climate policy, share three verified
collaborators, and attended the same conference — she may be an ideal research partner."*

```typescript
interface AIIntroduction {
  toUserId: string; suggestedUserId: string;
  evidenceCited: { type: 'shared_project'|'shared_collaborator'|'shared_event'|'complementary_skills'; refId: string }[];
}
// HARD RULE: an introduction with no evidenceCited entries is never generated —
// this is a stricter bar than §40.4's discovery ranking, which permits skill/trust
// signals alone. Introductions specifically require a *shared, verifiable* connection.
```

### 48.9 AI-Powered Collaborator Hub (extends §39.4 Candidate Matching Engine, §22 Messaging)

Users describe a need in plain language — *"I need a backend developer, a UI designer,
and a lawyer for a fintech project"* — and the same matching-signal set already defined
in §39.4 (skills, tools, portfolio quality, trust score, availability, location) ranks
verified candidates, with a plain-language rationale attached to every match, never a
bare score.

```typescript
interface CollaborationRequest {
  ownerId: string; projectDescription: string;
  rolesNeeded: { role: string; requiredSkills: Skill[]; minTrustScore?: number }[];
  matchedCandidates: { userId: string; rationale: string }[];
}
// Forming a team through this flow creates a Collab Hub project room (§22 Collaboration
// Hub) automatically — no separate team-formation system is created.
```

### 48.10 PROFILE Advisor — Personal AI Professional Agent (Flagship Differentiator)

```
NAMING RECONCILIATION (final — supersedes an earlier version of this note): this document
went through two naming passes. First, "Personal AI Professional Agent" (here) and "PROFILE
Advisor" (§50.9.2) were reconciled as one agent under the name PROFILE Advisor. Then a
third label, "PROFILE Copilot," was introduced at §37.26.11 scoped to Security — and rather
than discard that name, §48.0a resolves it correctly: **PROFILE Copilot™ is the platform's
umbrella name for its entire AI intelligence layer** (§48.0–§48.11 collectively), organised
into named skills (§48.0a's Copilot Skills Framework). PROFILE Advisor is that framework's
"Career Copilot" skill specifically — kept under its own established, free-tier,
user-facing name (referenced throughout §11.0, §18.5, §39.13, §50.9) rather than renamed,
since renaming it everywhere would carry far more risk than benefit. "Personal AI
Professional Agent" remains positioning language for PROFILE Advisor within that skill, not
a second product. See §48.0a for the complete resolution.
```

This is PROFILE's signature differentiator, not an incremental AI feature: every user has
a persistent AI agent — not a chatbot — that understands their career, evidence, and goals,
and acts as a lifelong professional operating companion. §50.9.2's `PROFILE_ADVISOR_EVALUATES`
is the user-facing subset of this same agent's output, delivered through the Daily Return
Engine (§11.0) — not a second, narrower agent running in parallel.

```typescript
interface PersonalAIAgentContext {
  userId: string;
  careerHistory: Activity[]; skills: Skill[];
  portfolio: PortfolioItemV3[]; cv: CVDocumentV3;
  collaborationHistory: Collaborator[]; documents: CredentialDocument[];
}
// This is PROFILE Advisor's full context object — §50.9.2 reads from this same interface,
// it does not define a separate one.

const AI_AGENT_CAPABILITIES = [
  'writes_and_refines_resumes_and_cover_letters',        // bounded by §48.0 no-fabrication rule
  'optimises_profile_and_trust_score_readiness',
  'drafts_proposals_and_grant_applications_from_own_evidence',
  'recommends_collaborators_and_mentors',                // feeds §48.9
  'finds_matching_jobs_scholarships_and_opportunities',  // feeds §18 Opportunity Hub
  'analyses_skill_gaps_and_proposes_a_learning_path',    // feeds §21 Learning & Communities
  'prepares_interview_briefs_and_summarises_messages',
  'drafts_routine_professional_messages_for_user_review',
  'explains_security_settings_and_recovery_options',      // §37.26.11 — same agent, Security-scoped
];

const AI_AGENT_HARD_RULES = [
  ...AI_ENGINE_PRINCIPLES,   // §48.0 — never fabricates, always labelled, user owns final output
  'never_shares_user_data_with_third_parties_without_explicit_per_action_consent',
  'user_can_fully_disable_the_agent_at_any_time_without_losing_any_platform_functionality',
];
```

The Agent is available as a persistent panel across the platform; a voice interface is
future-ready but not a launch dependency. It is deliberately built as a thin intelligence
layer over data every other module (§4, §11, §13, §47) already owns — it introduces no new
data store of its own.

### 48.11 PROFILE AI Gateway — Provider-Independent Architecture

```
No genuinely unlimited, zero-cost AI service exists at global scale — someone always pays
for compute. "Free to the user" (§32.1's profile_advisor_ai_assistant, §50.9.2) describes
pricing, not infrastructure. What protects that promise long-term is never depending on one
vendor's continued goodwill or pricing to keep it true.

Every AI-touching feature in this document — §48.1 through §48.10, PROFILE Advisor
(§50.9.2), CV/cover-letter drafting (§47), and verification/discovery/opportunity matching
(§16.4, §17.3, §18.4, §40.4) — calls through one internal interface, never a vendor SDK
directly:

    PROFILE feature → AI Gateway → routed model / provider
```

```typescript
const AI_GATEWAY_PRINCIPLES = [
  'no_feature_calls_a_model_providers_sdk_directly_—_every_call_goes_through_the_gateway',
  'provider_and_model_selection_is_configuration_not_code',
  'supports_open_or_self-hosted_models_commercial_APIs_and_specialised_models_side_by_side',
  'provider_can_be_swapped_load-balanced_or_multi-sourced_without_touching_CV_portfolio_or_
   opportunity-matching_feature_code',
  'per-feature_routing_is_allowed_—_e.g._verification_matching_(§16.4)_may_route_to_a_
   different_model_than_CV_drafting_(§47)',
  'every_gateway_call_is_logged_to_the_same_audit_trail_already_required_by_§48.5_and_§48.6_—
   not_a_second_logging_system',
];
// Open/self-hosted models are a legitimate routing target for development, cost control, or
// data-residency requirements — the Gateway is what makes that a configuration change, not
// a rebuild. This section adds no new AI capability of its own; it is the seam that keeps
// every capability in §48.1–§48.10 swappable underneath.
```

# 49. PROTOTYPE AUDIT & CONSOLIDATION

### 49.0 Audit Scope & Method

This section applies the Non-Destructive Execution Rule (per the Continuation Directive) to
the current `profile-mvp.jsx` prototype. It was produced by comparing the working build against
Master Prompt v2.0 and Patch v3.2 line-by-line. Nothing here proposes a rebuild — every item
is either a **missing page** (build it, once, correctly) or a **cosmetic feature masking absent
logic** (wire it to real state). No working code identified below is to be rewritten from
scratch; it is extended in place, consistent with LAW 1 and LAW 2 of the Patch Law (top of doc).

### 49.1 Missing Pages & Modules (Build, Not Yet Started)

```typescript
const MISSING_PAGES = [
  { route: '/profile/@username', spec: '§24.1',              status: 'not_implemented' },
  { route: '/analytics',          spec: '§23, §40.9',         status: 'not_implemented' },
  { route: '/trust',              spec: '§12, §16',           status: 'not_implemented' },
  { route: '/opportunities',      spec: '§18, §39',           status: 'not_implemented' },
  { route: '/admin',              spec: '§26, §37.11',        status: 'not_implemented' },
  { route: '/learn',              spec: '§21',                status: 'not_implemented' },
  { route: '/collab',             spec: '§22',                status: 'not_implemented' },
];
// Every route above must resolve to a real page before any further feature work —
// this is the Route Integrity Engine requirement below (§49.3) applied to real gaps.
```

### 49.2 Present But Non-Functional (Fix In Place, Do Not Rebuild Surrounding Code)

| Component | Symptom | Required Fix | Spec Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Onboarding photo/cover upload | Fires a toast only; no file stored or rendered | Wire to real upload pipeline with persistence + render | §37 image system, §47.9 |
| Settings "Change photo" | Decorative button, no handler | Same upload pipeline as above, shared component | §37.9 |
| CV Page | Renders unconditionally for every account type | Gate behind `accountType === 'individual'`; render `OrganisationPortfolioDocument` otherwise | §15.2, §49.5 below |
| CV template switcher | Highlights selection; underlying layout never changes | Bind selected template to actual layout renderer, one per §47.5 template | §47.5 |
| Trust Score ring (Home) | Hardcoded `80` and hardcoded `strokeDashoffset` | Compute from `TrustScore.total` per §13 formula | §13 |
| "People to connect" panel (Home) | Separate hardcoded array duplicating Discover's dataset | Replace with a scoped query into the same discovery source Discover uses | §17, §40.4 |
| Tool metadata detection (Post Modal) | `Math.random()` picks from a fixed map — fabricated, not detected | Replace with real file-metadata extraction, or clearly label as manual tool tagging until built | §48.0 (AI Engine — no fabricated output) |

### 49.3 Route Integrity Engine (formalizes the Continuation Directive's routing requirement)

```typescript
interface RouteIntegrityCheck {
  registeredRoute: string;
  hasComponent:    boolean;
  fallbackBehavior: 'load_feature_shell_never_404';
}
// RULE: a registered nav item with no page yet must render a feature shell
// (header + "this section is being finished" empty state per §35 Empty State Rule),
// NEVER a 404. §49.1's missing pages should show shells today, real pages next.
const ROUTE_INTEGRITY_RULE = 'No registered navigation item may ever resolve to Page Not Found.';
```

### 49.4 Account-Type State Wiring

```typescript
// GAP: Step 1 of onboarding captures account type and it is never read again anywhere
// in the app. This single gap is why CV renders for organisations (§49.2) and why
// no module can branch correctly by account type.

interface AccountContextV3 {
  accountType: 'individual' | 'organisation';
  pathway?:    'professional' | 'competence' | 'hybrid';   // individual only, §8.3 — corrected
                                                              // from 'academic' to 'professional' to match
                                                              // the canonical union in §4.1's User schema;
                                                              // same field, was drifting into a second
                                                              // literal type across two sections
  orgType?:    OrgType;                                 // organisation only, §4.2
}
// RULE: AccountContext must be established at Step 1 of onboarding and read by
// every module that branches on account type — CV/Portfolio (§15.2), nav (§49.6),
// avatar dropdown contents, Settings tabs, and Hiring Dashboard visibility (§37.13).
```

### 49.5 Organisation Portfolio Document Schema (closes a real spec gap, not just a code gap)

```typescript
// Patch v3.2 §47.2 extended CVDocument for individuals but never gave organisations
// an equivalent typed schema — §15.2 says organisations get "Dynamic Portfolio ONLY"
// but no interface existed for it until now.

interface OrganisationPortfolioDocument {
  id:             string;
  orgId:          string;                 // OPID
  version:        number;
  templateId:     string;                 // one of the 10 templates in §15.3 (org-appropriate subset)
  isAIGenerated:  boolean;
  isEditable:     true;

  sections: {
    about:              CVSection<OrgAboutContent>;
    programmesInitiatives: CVSection<ProgrammeEntry[]>;
    staffHighlights:    CVSection<StaffHighlightEntry[]>;   // verified staff only, §24.2
    publications:       CVSection<PublicationEntry[]>;
    communityImpact:    CVSection<ImpactRecord[]>;
    partnerships:       CVSection<PartnershipEntry[]>;
    activeJobListings:  CVSection<JobListing[]>;            // live pull from §4.9
    services:           CVSection<ProfessionalServiceListing[]>; // §38.1
    trustScore:         CVSection<TrustScorePanel>;
    verificationAuthorityStatus: CVSection<VerificationAuthorityBadge>;
  };

  visibility:     SectionVisibility;
  livePreview:    boolean;
  qrCode:         string;
  recruiterView:  boolean;
  lastUpdated:    Date;
}
// HARD RULE (restated from §15.2 and the Continuation Directive): organisations
// NEVER receive a CVDocument or CVDocumentV3 instance. The CV page component must
// be structurally incapable of rendering for accountType === 'organisation' —
// not just visually hidden.
```

### 49.6 Navigation Consolidation Rule

```typescript
// GAP: topbar gear icon and avatar circle both open Settings — two paths to one
// destination, and no single config currently drives both mobile and desktop nav.

const NAV_CONFIG_SINGLE_SOURCE = {
  primaryNav: ['home', 'connections', 'create_activity', 'opportunity', 'cv_or_portfolio'], // §10.1, superseded from the original discover-inclusive draft
  topRight:   ['notifications', 'messages', 'avatar', 'hamburger'],
  avatarDropdown: 'see PROFILE_DRAWER_ITEMS §10.3 — quick access only',
  hamburgerMenu: 'see HAMBURGER_MENU_ITEMS §10.4 — full secondary module list',
};
// RULE: mobile and desktop nav render from this single config, not two hand-maintained
// component trees. Gear icon is removed; avatar opens the Profile Drawer, hamburger opens
// the full ecosystem menu — never redundant paths to the same destination.
```

### 49.7 Messaging Engine Completion Requirement

```typescript
// GAP: current messaging is plain text only. §20 MessageType already defines the
// full set — this is a build-completion item, not a new schema.
const MESSAGE_TYPES_TO_IMPLEMENT = [
  'file', 'document', 'proposal', 'contract', 'schedule_card',
  'work_submission', 'assignment_submission',   // NEW — see below
];
// Ship in this order: file/document sharing first (lowest complexity, highest
// immediate value), then schedule_card (interview scheduling), then proposal/contract
// (needed for §38.1 Professional Services negotiation flows), then work_submission and
// assignment_submission (needed for remote-work deliverables and coursework below).

// NEW message and conversation types — messaging is explicitly not casual-chat-only:
// 'work_submission' carries a deliverable (file/document/link bundle) against an agreed
// scope (§38.1 service, §18 hiring, or §39 recruitment context) with a client/employer
// accept-or-request-changes response, mirroring §42.3's accept/reject pattern rather than
// inventing a second approval flow.
// 'assignment_submission' is the same mechanism in an education context — a mentor,
// instructor, or supervisor (§21.2 Mentorship, §50.9.7 Academy) receiving and confirming
// coursework — and, on confirmation, can become a normal Activity record (§4.3) exactly
// like §38.6c's confirmed commerce transactions do.
// 'education' is now part of ConversationType itself (§20), alongside the existing
// 'hiring' | 'collaboration' | 'verification' | 'partnership' | 'consulting' |
// 'marketplace' — coursework and mentorship threads get their own lane, not folded
// into 'collaboration' where they'd be hard to filter.
```

### 49.7a Camera, Document Scanning & Barcode Capture (extends §11.4 Record Work
Composer, §20 Messaging attachments, §4.1 profilePhoto/coverPhoto — one capture layer,
used everywhere media is uploaded, not rebuilt per feature)

```typescript
const DEVICE_CAPTURE_CAPABILITIES = {
  photoCapture: {
    usedBy: ['profilePhoto', 'coverPhoto', 'activity_media_(§11.4)', 'message_attachments_(§20)', 'marketplace_listing_photos_(§19)'],
    behavior: 'direct camera access, not just a file picker — capture and upload in one step',
  },
  documentScan: {
    // Multi-page scan-to-PDF, not a single raw photo of a document:
    behavior: ['edge_detection', 'perspective_correction', 'multi_page_capture_into_one_file',
               'export_as_PDF_or_image_(matches_§11.4_COMPOSER_UPLOAD_SPEC_accepted_types)'],
    usedBy: ['credential_uploads_(§12.5_Credential_Vault)', 'activity_evidence_(§4.3)',
             'work_submission_and_assignment_submission_(§49.7)', 'contract_signing_evidence_(§38.1)'],
  },
  barcodeAndQRScan: {
    behavior: 'high-resolution scan with the same camera layer, not a separate module',
    usedBy: [
      'scanning_someone_elses_Trust_Passport_QR_(§12.6)_to_pull_up_their_verified_profile_—_e.g._at_a_career_fair_or_interview',
      'scanning_a_products_barcode_when_creating_a_Marketplace_physical-goods_listing_(§19)_to_auto-fill_product_details',
      'scanning_a_verification_QR_(§12.4)_to_confirm_a_credential_on_the_spot',
    ],
  },
};
// This is a capture layer, not a new upload pipeline — every file it produces still flows
// through the existing COMPOSER_UPLOAD_SPEC (§11.4), Copyright System (§11.4), and virus/
// malware scanning (§27.2) exactly as a regular upload would.
```

### 49.8 Fabricated-Data Prohibition (ties to §48.0 AI Engine governance)

```
RULE: no component may present randomly-generated or hardcoded output as if it were
detected, computed, or AI-derived (e.g. PostModal's Math.random() tool-metadata
picker, Home's static Trust Score). Where the real pipeline isn't built yet, the UI
must either omit the feature or clearly label it manual/placeholder-pending — silent
fabrication is a Continuation Directive violation, not a cosmetic shortcut.
```

### 49.9 Recommended Build Sequence

```
1. Route Integrity Engine (§49.3)       — unblocks safe testing of everything else
2. AccountContext wiring (§49.4)         — unblocks correct CV/Portfolio branching
3. My Profile page (§24.1)               — highest-visibility reported gap
4. Image upload pipeline (§49.2)         — small scope, high perceived-polish impact
5. Organisation Portfolio rendering (§49.5) — closes the CV-for-orgs violation
6. Analytics page (§23, real computed data, not fabricated)
7. Trust Center (§12, §16)               — largest build; everything downstream depends on it
```

# 50. TRUST ECOSYSTEM INTEGRITY, GOVERNANCE & RELEASE DISCIPLINE

### 50.0 Consolidation Note

This section is the single capstone module for everything that governs trust integrity,
security, launch discipline, and release safety across PROFILE. It replaces what were
previously drafted as separate Sections 50–57 — nothing from those drafts was dropped;
they are re-homed here as subsections (50.1–50.5) so the document ends at §50 rather than
sprawling further. It also adds, for the first time, the **Institution & Organization Trust
Registry (IOTR)**, the **ecosystem risk catalog**, the **PROFILE Trust Timeline**, and the
**Enterprise Workflow Schema Engine** — each checked against Sections 1–49 first and merged
into an existing home wherever one existed, per Patch Law 5.

```
POSITIONING PRINCIPLE (governs all of §50):
PROFILE is never marketed as "a platform that catches fake certificates."
PROFILE is the global infrastructure that enables trusted verification — faster,
cheaper, auditable, and globally accessible. PROFILE never declares a claim true
or false. It records the claim, routes it to the authorized issuing entity, and
preserves the outcome. The issuing institution is always the authority. This is
restated from §16.5's Institutional Review Console and now applies platform-wide.
```

---

### 50.1 Trust Score Governance, Dispute, Appeal & Risk Infrastructure

*(Consolidates former §50 in full — content unchanged, re-homed here.)*

```typescript
// GOVERNANCE — extends §13
interface TrustScoreFormulaChangeLog {
  version: string; changedBy: string; changeReason: string;
  weightsBefore: typeof TRUST_WEIGHTS; weightsAfter: typeof TRUST_WEIGHTS;
  effectiveDate: Date; historicalRecomputeAvailable: true;
}
const TRUST_GOVERNANCE_POLICY = {
  whoCanChangeWeights: 'compliance_officer + super_admin only, dual sign-off required',
  reviewCadence: 'quarterly, or immediately after a confirmed dispute pattern',
  biasAndFairnessReview: 'required before any weight change ships, documented in the change log',
  newSignalAdditionProcess: 'proposed → reviewed against §13.2 weight table → versioned → published',
};
// DISCLOSURE RULE (new, from risk catalog #18): users see category-level standing
// ("Identity: Excellent", "Verification: Very Strong"), never the underlying formula.
interface TrustCategorySummary {
  identity: 'Excellent'|'Strong'|'Growing'|'Weak';
  experience: 'Excellent'|'Strong'|'Growing'|'Weak';
  evidence: 'Excellent'|'Strong'|'Growing'|'Weak';
  verification: 'Excellent'|'Strong'|'Growing'|'Weak';
  communityTrust: 'Excellent'|'Strong'|'Growing'|'Weak';
}
```

```typescript
// DISPUTE & APPEAL — extends §28.4
interface Dispute {
  id: string; userId: string;
  disputeType: 'verification_decision'|'trust_score_change'|'recruiter_filter_exclusion'|'moderation_action'|'institutional_rejection';
  targetId: string; reason: string; evidence: MediaAsset[];
  status: 'submitted'|'under_review'|'upheld'|'corrected'|'removed'|'escalated'|'independent_review';
  respondedBy?: string; decisionRationale?: string; submittedAt: Date; resolvedAt?: Date;
  appealWindow: '14_days';
}
// Extends risk #22 (Institutional Abuse): an institution wrongly rejecting a valid
// credential is disputable through this same object, with an independent-review
// escalation path where the institution itself is the disputed party.
// Extends risk #25 (Reputation Recovery): corrections, re-verification, and updated
// credentials are handled as Dispute outcomes ('corrected'), never permanent penalties.
const DISPUTE_ENTRY_POINTS = ['verification_outcome_screen','major_trust_score_change_notification','recruiter_filter_exclusion_explanation','institutional_rejection_notice'];
```

```typescript
// ABUSE & FRAUD DETECTION — extends §27.3, absorbs the full risk catalog (items 7–17)
const RISK_SIGNALS = [
  'unusual_verification_pattern', 'trust_score_spike_anomaly',
  'repeated_disputes_around_one_institution_or_user', 'abnormal_messaging_pattern',
  'marketplace_fraud_indicator',                    // risk #16 Marketplace Scams
  'fake_job_posting_pattern',                        // risk #17 — requires recruiter/company identity verification
  'circular_endorsement_or_employment_cluster',      // risk #10 Fraud Rings — "Trust Graph Intelligence"
  'impossible_timeline_overlap',                     // risk #11 Time Conflicts
  'unrealistic_progression_flag',                    // risk #12 — flags for human review, never auto-penalizes
  'unreferenced_project_claim',                      // risk #13 Fake Projects — evidence-scored, not binary
  'tool_attribution_without_certification',          // risk #14 — see Badge separation below
  'declared_only_skill_pattern',                      // risk #15 Fake Skills
  'document_hash_mismatch_after_upload',             // risk #9 AI-Generated/Altered Certificates
  'mass_scraping_pattern',                           // NEW (§37 security patch) — abnormal request
                                                      // volume/pattern against public profile or
                                                      // search endpoints, rate-limited per §37.1
  'bot_account_signals',                             // NEW — registration/activity velocity and
                                                      // behavioural patterns inconsistent with a
                                                      // human user, routed to §28.4 enforcement
  'stolen_portfolio_reupload',                        // NEW — extends §11.4's existing duplicate-
                                                      // upload/content-hash detection to flag the
                                                      // same media re-uploaded under a different
                                                      // account, not just duplicate uploads by one
];
const RISK_RESPONSES = ['automatic_soft_flag','rate_limit','temporary_trust_freeze','manual_review_queue','require_additional_MFA_factor_for_remainder_of_session'];
const ADMIN_RISK_AND_INTEGRITY_MODULE = {
  route: '/admin/risk-integrity',
  shows: ['flagged_signals','active_freezes','review_queue','resolution_history'],
  accessRole: 'compliance_officer | super_admin',
};

// Risk #7 Fake Referees — extends §40.5 PROFILE References
interface ReferenceInvitation {
  referrerContact: string; mustVerifyOwnIdentityFirst: true;   // no reference accepted pre-identity-check
  status: 'invited'|'identity_verified'|'reference_submitted';
}
// Risk #8 Paid Fake Reviews — reaffirms §40.5: recommendations must link to an
// employer, institution, client, project, contract, or verified shared activity.
// An unlinked recommendation cannot exist.

// Risk #9 Document Integrity — extends §4.7 CredentialDocument
interface DocumentIntegrityRecord {
  documentFingerprint: string; contentHash: string; metadataSnapshot: object;
  issueTimestamp: Date; verificationTimestamp: Date; institutionSignature: string;
  invalidatesTrustOnChange: true;   // any post-upload modification voids prior verification
}

// Risk #14 Tool Attribution Abuse — extends §4.8 Tool Schema badge model
type ToolRelationshipBadge = 'uses_tool' | 'certified_user' | 'official_partner' | 'trainer';
// Tagging a tool is never itself an endorsement claim — the badge type makes the
// distinction explicit and each higher tier requires its own verification path.

// Risk #15 Fake Skills — extends §4.1 Skill handling
// This is PROFILE's Verified Expertise Framework: skills are never a flat, binary claim.
// Confidence rises only through evidence, never through self-declaration alone.
type SkillConfidenceLevel = 'declared'|'evidence_backed'|'verified'|'certified'|'frequently_used';
// Verification of a skill at the 'verified' tier or above may combine AI-assisted
// practical assessment, employer validation, peer endorsement tied to real
// collaboration history (§40.5 PROFILE References), and project evidence
// (§4.3 Activity) — never cosmetic status alone. Displayed on CV/Portfolio (§47) as a
// visible tier badge next to each skill, not a single undifferentiated skill tag.
```

### 50.2 Security Threat Model & Data Domain Separation

```typescript
const THREAT_MODEL = ['account_takeover','identity_theft','verification_fraud','trust_score_manipulation','insider_abuse','marketplace_and_messaging_abuse'];

const MFA_POLICY_BY_RISK_TIER = {
  standard_individual: 'any method in §37.3 MFA_METHODS acceptable',
  organisation_admin: 'authenticator_app or passkey required; SMS labelled as weaker fallback only',
  institution_verifier: 'authenticator_app or passkey required; SMS labelled as weaker fallback only',
  super_admin: 'phishing_resistant_hardware_backed_MFA required (passkey/WebAuthn)',
};

interface ScopedAccessToken {
  featureArea: 'trust_center'|'admin_center'|'vxi'|'marketplace'|'messaging';
  role: OrgAdminRoleV3; scope: 'read'|'write'|'approve'; leastPrivilege: true;
}

interface TrustIntegrityLedgerEntry {
  eventType: 'trust_score_change'|'verification_lifecycle'|'admin_action'|'dispute_decision';
  refId: string; timestamp: Date; appendOnly: true; storedSeparatelyFrom: 'general_security_audit_log';
}

const DATA_DOMAINS = {
  publicIdentityLayer: { contains: ['profile','public_activities','high_level_trust_signals'], audience: 'recruiters_and_public_per_visibility_settings_§25' },
  privateEvidenceLayer: { contains: ['raw_documents','detailed_verification_identifiers_§12.3_UVIF','internal_logs'], audience: 'owner_and_authorised_verifiers_only' },
  institutionalArtifactsLayer: { contains: ['verification_schemas_§16.2','credential_datasets_§16.3','review_queues','internal_notes'], audience: 'institution_staff_with_assigned_role_only' },
};

// Risk #23 Vendor & Third-Party Integrations — extends §16.7 VXI APIs
interface ScopedAPIGrant {
  consumer: 'bank'|'immigration_authority'|'university'|'government';
  returnsOnly: 'minimum_verified_attributes_required'; neverReturns: 'full_user_record';
}
```

```typescript
  'cv_portfolio_autobuild_and_edit', 'discover_trust_based_search',
  'opportunity_hub_basic_browsing_and_applications', 'messaging_for_approved_workflows',
  'admin_centre_essentials', 'security_performance_accessibility_offline_legal_support_pages',
];

// HOLD-BACK LIST — hidden, internal-only, or role-gated until activated per the rule below
const V1_HOLD_BACK_LIST = [
  'marketplace', 'learning_and_communities', 'collab_hub', 'advanced_analytics_for_end_users',
  'public_trust_leaderboards_or_ranking_surfaces', 'provider_usage_signals_and_partner_intelligence',
  'monetisation_beyond_basic_subscription_billing', 'vanity_or_engagement_loop_mechanics',
  'automation_that_changes_trust_or_access_without_human_review_fallback',
  'any_feature_with_unresolved_legal_policy_or_data_governance_questions',
];

interface FeatureFlag {
  module: string; environment: 'dev'|'staging'|'production';
  scope: 'all_users' | 'admin_only' | 'institution_only' | 'region' | 'country'
       | 'organisation' | 'user_role' | 'subscription_tier' | 'beta_group';
  defaultState: 'off_in_production_until_explicitly_enabled';
}

// ACTIVATION RULE — a hold-back feature may go live only after ALL of:
const ACTIVATION_GATE = ['functional_test_coverage','security_review','abuse_review','accessibility_review','rollback_plan','data_retention_and_deletion_policy','clear_empty_state_ux','explicit_owner_sign_off'];

];

const COPY_DISCIPLINE = { tone: 'practical over grandiose', appliesTo: 'all core-loop UX copy; §44 Brand Voice already requires this' };

  onAccountDeactivation: 'trust passport data frozen, not deleted, pending reactivation window',

};
const TOOL_PROVIDER_SIGNAL_GATE = { status: 'off_by_default', activatesOnlyAfter: 'signed commercial and legal agreement per provider' };
```

---

### 50.4 Institution & Organization Trust Registry (IOTR) & Enterprise Workflow Schema Engine

*(New — closes the largest remaining gap: nothing in §1–49 defined how an organization's*
*identity, history, or bulk-processing capability is represented before or after it joins.)*

**Routing decision:** IOTR is owned by the **Verification** module (extends §16 Institutional
Trust Hub); the Workflow Schema Engine is owned by the **Opportunity** module (extends §18)
but is deliberately generalized so Verification, Admin, and Marketplace can reuse it —
per Patch Law 5, it lives in one place (Opportunity) and is imported elsewhere, never rebuilt.

```typescript
// IOTR — extends §16.2 Institutional Schema Builder with the entity layer it assumed but never defined
type OrgRegistryLevel = 'verified' | 'recognized' | 'user_submitted' | 'rejected';

interface OrgRegistryEntry {
  id: string;                          // OPID once claimed; provisional ID before claim
  registryLevel: OrgRegistryLevel;
  currentName: string;
  formerNames: string[];               // e.g. "University of Ife" → "Obafemi Awolowo University"
  aliases: string[];
  mergerHistory: string[];
  acquisitionHistory: string[];
  officialRegistrationNumber?: string;
  country: string;
  activePeriod: { from: Date; to?: Date };  // supports closed/defunct entities
  claimedBy?: string;                  // PID of verified authorized representative, once claimed
}

// SMART ORGANIZATION SEARCH — prevents duplicate entities at entry time
interface OrgMatchSuggestion { candidateId: string; matchType: 'exact'|'former_name'|'alias'|'acronym'|'regional_spelling'; confidence: number; }
// User is always shown suggestions before a new provisional org is created — extends §16.4 Matching Engine's
// name-resolution logic (already handles Mohammed/Muhammad-style variance) to organization names.

// CLAIMS, NOT FACTS — extends §4.3 Activity institutions/organisations display logic
type AffiliationDisplayState = 'claimed_education'|'claimed_employment'|'claimed_membership'|'claimed_certification'|'verified_by_institution';
// A claim never renders as a fact until an OrgRegistryEntry at 'verified' level confirms it.

// ORGANIZATION CLAIMING — extends §7.4 (org onboarding) with the verification gate IOTR requires
interface OrgClaimRequest {
  orgRegistryEntryId: string; officialEmailDomainVerified: boolean;
  legalRegistrationDocuments: MediaAsset[]; representativeIdentityVerified: boolean;
  approvedBy: 'profile_trust_operations'; onApproval: 'all_queued_user_claims_become_eligible_for_verification';
}

// TRUST STATUS DISPLAY
const ORG_TRUST_STATUS_INDICATORS = {
  verified: '🟢 Verified Organisation',
  recognized: '🟡 Recognized — Verification Not Yet Available',
  user_submitted: '🔵 Awaiting Organisational Claim',
  rejected: '🔴 Restricted / Removed — hidden from public search, retained in audit logs only',
};

// DORMANT & NON-RESPONSIVE INSTITUTION HANDLING — extends §12.4 Verification Timeline
type VerificationOutcomeV2 = 'verified'|'pending_institution'|'institution_not_yet_connected'|'alternative_verification_available'|'records_unavailable'|'records_destroyed'|'manual_archive_search_required'|'unable_to_verify';
// When a previously unconnected institution later joins (via OrgClaimRequest), every
// queued credential referencing it automatically re-enters the verification queue — no user re-submission required.

// INSTITUTION FEE WORKFLOW — extends §38.9 Institution Revenue
const INSTITUTION_VERIFICATION_FEE_WORKFLOW = ['user_submits_credential','institution_receives_request','institution_sets_fee_if_any','user_pays_institution','institution_verifies','profile_records_verification_and_commission_if_applicable'];

// CLOSED COMPANY ALTERNATIVE EVIDENCE — extends §4.3 Activity evidence model
const ALTERNATIVE_EMPLOYMENT_EVIDENCE = ['employment_contract','tax_records','payslips','colleague_verification','former_hr_contact','court_records','professional_body_confirmation'];
interface AlternativeEvidenceConfidenceScore { evidenceTypeCount: number; crossCorroborated: boolean; confidenceLevel: 'low'|'moderate'|'high'; }
```

```typescript
// ENTERPRISE WORKFLOW SCHEMA ENGINE — extends §18 Opportunity Hub, generalized for reuse
// One engine, many uses: recruitment, admissions, licensing, accreditation, scholarships,
// immigration credential checks, vendor onboarding, membership renewal, grant review.

interface WorkflowSchema {
  id: string; ownerId: string;                 // OPID of the org/institution/government running it
  workflowType: 'recruitment'|'admissions'|'licensing'|'accreditation'|'scholarship'|'immigration_check'|'vendor_onboarding'|'membership_renewal'|'grant_review'|'custom';
  name: string; targetVolume: number; applicationWindow: { from: Date; to: Date };
  requiredDocuments: CredentialDocType[];
  requiredSkills: Skill[];
  minimumTrustScore?: number;
  requiredVerifications: VerificationType[];
  stages: WorkflowStage[];
  reviewers: string[];                          // PIDs with 'approve' scope per §50.2 ScopedAccessToken
  finalApprover: string;
  autoOfferGeneration: boolean;
}

interface WorkflowStage {
  name: string;                                  // e.g. 'Applied','Eligible','Shortlisted','Assessment','Interview','Offer','Accepted','Onboarding'
  order: number;
  bulkActionsSupported: ['approve','reject','request_more_information'];
}

// BULK RECORD INGESTION — reuses §16.3 Bulk Record Ingestion Engine exactly (SUPPORTED_FORMATS,
// RECORD_TYPES) rather than duplicating it; a WorkflowSchema's requiredDocuments/requiredVerifications
// are matched against the same institution dataset §16.3 already ingests.

interface BulkCredentialMatch {
  workflowId?: string;                           // present when matching is happening inside a workflow context
  matchedAutomatically: boolean;                 // reuses §16.4 Matching Engine's confidence scoring
  requiresManualReview: boolean;
}

const BULK_ACTIONS = ['bulk_messaging_by_stage','bulk_document_request_by_stage','bulk_trust_decision_review'];
// Bulk messaging and document requests deliver into each candidate's individual Messaging
// thread and Trust Center respectively — no separate bulk-only inbox is created (Patch Law 5).

const WORKFLOW_ANALYTICS = ['applications_received','verification_rate','completion_rate','average_trust_score','most_common_skills','institution_distribution','country_distribution','drop_off_rate','hiring_conversion','time_to_hire'];
// Surfaces inside the existing Hiring Dashboard (§37.13) and Organisation Analytics (§40.9) —
// not a new analytics surface.
```

### 50.5 PROFILE Trust Timeline

*(New — extends §12.6 Trust Passport with the chronological ledger view it implied but never modeled.)*

```typescript
interface TrustTimelineEntry {
  id: string; userId: string;                    // or orgId for organisation timelines
  date: Date;
  eventType: 'credential_uploaded'|'credential_verified'|'employment_confirmed'|'project_completed'|'license_verified'|'testimonial_received'|'publication_linked'|'dispute_resolved'|'trust_score_milestone';
  refId: string;                                  // links back into TrustIntegrityLedgerEntry (§50.2)
  summary: string;                                 // e.g. "University of Lagos verified BSc Industrial Design"
}
// Rendered as a chronological ledger on the Trust Passport (§12.6) and public profile
// (§24.1), giving recruiters, institutions, and clients a trusted history rather than
// a single snapshot badge — this is the same data already captured in TrustHistoryEntry
// (§13) and TrustIntegrityLedgerEntry (§50.2), presented as one unified view, not a new
// data store.
```

```
pass — Preservation, Feature Routing, System Validation, Security, Media, Community,
```
